=\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})^{G}}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}(\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho,F}\cdot\mathbf{a}^{E}\nabla\psi^{E}_{m}-\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho,F}\cdot q)\,{\mathrm{d}}(\mu+\mu_{F\cap G})\,\biggl|\,\mu\mathbin{\vrule height=6.02773pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=4.30554pt}{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})^{c}},\,\mu({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})=n\right].

Notice now that the integrals above give the same contribution for every particle in
{(\mu+\mu_{F\cap G})}
, because
\psi_{m}^{\rho,F},\psi^{E}_{m}
and
\mathbf{a}^{E}
are all invariant under permutations for these particles. As a consequence, we have

\displaystyle B_{n}(\mu_{\rho},\mu\mathbin{\vrule height=6.02773pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=4.30554pt}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})^{c},\mu_{F\setminus E},\mu_{E\setminus G})

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{n}{n+|F\cap G|}\right)\widetilde{B}_{n}(\mu_{\rho},\mu\mathbin{\vrule height=6.02773pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=4.30554pt}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})^{c},\mu_{F\setminus E},\mu_{E\setminus G})

\displaystyle=0.

We thus established (
3.25
). Then we put it back to (
3.23
) and (
3.24
), which implies that the left-hand side of (
3.23
) is zero and concludes the proof of (
3.21
).

We now turn to (
3.22
). The proof is similar and one can repeat the 4 steps above. The only difference is that we also need to do the expansion according to the number of particles
\mu_{\rho}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})
and we skip the details.
∎

Remark 3.4
.

The proof in fact yields the following stronger result: for all finite sets
E,F\subseteq\mathbb{N}_{+}
, and
G\subseteq E
, we have that

\displaystyle\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{E\cup F}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}(\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho,F}\cdot\mathbf{a}^{E}\nabla\psi^{E}_{m}-\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho,F}\cdot q)\,{\mathrm{d}}(\mu+\mu_{G})\right]=0,

\displaystyle\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{E\cup F}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}(\nabla\psi_{m}^{F}\cdot\mathbf{a}^{\rho,E}\nabla\psi^{\rho,E}_{m}-\nabla\psi_{m}^{F}\cdot q)\,{\mathrm{d}}(\mu+\mu_{G})\right]=0.

Remark 3.5
.

We point out that, choosing
\rho=0
in (
3.21
), we recover the same identity with
\psi_{m}^{\rho,F}
replaced by
\psi_{m}^{F}
. From this, we may also change the density of the distribution of
\mu
from
\rho_{0}
to
\rho_{0}+\rho
, and obtain the analogue of (
3.22
) with
\psi_{m}^{F}
replaced by
\psi_{m}^{\rho,F}
.

Finally, we note that, by linearity, (
3.21
) and (
3.22
) are also true if we use test functions of the form
D_{F}\psi_{m}^{G\setminus F}
or
D_{F}\psi_{m}^{\rho,G\setminus F}
,
F,G\subseteq\mathbb{N}
and we replace the outer integrals by
\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{E\cup F\cup G}}
.

4.
First-order differentiability

In this section we prove Theorem
2.1
. We explain at first its main ingredient, and it also gives us the opportunity to exemplify the sort of arguments that will be generalized later to obtain Theorem
2.3
.

We recall that we have fixed a vector
q\in{\mathbb{R}^{d}}
, and that
\psi_{m}
denotes the optimizer in the definition (
2.3
) of the quantity
\nu_{*}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},q,\rho_{0})
. We use the notation
\lesssim
for
\leqslant C\times
with the constant
C
depending only
d
,
\Lambda
and the length of the vector
q\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

The quantity that we will study is the difference between the diffusion coefficients at different densities

(4.1)

\displaystyle\Delta^{\rho}(\rho_{0}):=q\cdot{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}^{-1}(\rho_{0}+\rho)q-q\cdot{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}^{-1}(\rho_{0})q,

as well as, for each
m\in\mathbb{N}
, its finite-volume analogue

(4.2)

\displaystyle\Delta^{\rho}_{m}(\rho_{0}):=q\cdot{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}_{*}^{-1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{0}+\rho)q-q\cdot{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}_{*}^{-1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{0})q.

In order to prove Theorem
2.1
, we first establish its finite-volume version for
\Delta^{\rho}_{m}(\rho_{0})
, with estimates that hold uniformly over
m
, and then pass to the limit. We recall that the notation
\psi_{m}^{\{1\}},\mathbf{a}^{\{1\}}
is defined in (
2.9
).

Proposition 4.1
.

For any
\rho,\rho_{0}>0
fixed, it holds

(4.3)

\displaystyle\Delta^{\rho}(\rho_{0})=\lim_{m\to\infty}\Delta^{\rho}_{m}(\rho_{0}).

Moreover, uniformly over
m\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

(4.4)

\displaystyle|\Delta^{\rho}_{m}(\rho_{0})-c_{1,m}(\rho_{0})\rho|\lesssim\rho^{2},

with

(4.5)

\displaystyle c_{1,m}(\rho_{0}):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi_{m}\cdot(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{\{1\}})\nabla\psi_{m}^{\{1\}}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}.

The proof of Proposition
4.1
relies on two ingredients. The first is the following representation formula for the difference term
\Delta_{m}^{\rho}(\rho_{0})
.

Lemma 4.2
.

For every
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
\rho>0
, we have

(4.6)

\displaystyle\Delta_{m}^{\rho}(\rho_{0})=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi_{m}\cdot(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{\rho})\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right].

One may find that (
4.6
) and (
4.5
) look quite similar, which explains that
c_{1,m}(\rho_{0})
is indeed its first order approximation. To verify this approximation, we also need some estimate, which is the second ingredient for the proof of Proposition
4.1
. The next lemma allows us to compare the behavior of the optimizers
\psi_{m},\psi_{m}^{\rho}
when the measures
\mu
or
\mu+\mu_{\rho}
are perturbed by one or two additional particles. Given
E\subseteq\mathbb{N}_{+}
, we recall the definitions (
2.10
) and (
2.11
) for the finite difference
D_{E}
, and the notation for the integrals
\int_{U^{E}}
in (
3.19
) .

Lemma 4.3
.

For every
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
E,F\subseteq\mathbb{N}_{+}
with
|E|\leqslant 2
and
|F|\leqslant 1
, we have

(4.7)

\displaystyle\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{E}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}|\nabla D_{E}\psi_{m}|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\lesssim 1,

(4.8)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\left|\int_{(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{F}}\nabla D_{F}\psi_{m}\,\right|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\lesssim 1.

Remark 4.4
.

Applying Lemma
4.3
for an underlying particle density of
\rho_{0}+\rho
instead of
\rho
, we see that the same estimates as in (
4.7
) and (
4.8
) hold if we replace
\psi_{m}
by
\psi_{m}^{\rho}
and
\mu
by
\mu+\mu_{\rho}
.

Proposition
4.1
and Lemma
4.3
will be generalized in Section
5
to prove Theorem
2.3
, where a higher-order approximation is needed. Estimate (
4.7
), indeed, corresponds to Proposition
5.4
with
|F|\leqslant 2,G=\emptyset
, while (
4.8
) corresponds to
F=G
with
|F|=1
.

We organize the remainder of this section as follows. We finish the introduction with a lemma gathering some basic properties of Poisson point processes. In Subsection
4.1
, we prove Lemmas
4.2
and
4.3
. Then we devote Subsection
4.2
to the proof of the key result Proposition
4.1
. Subsection
4.3
builds upon Proposition
4.1
to conclude for the validity of Theorem
2.1
.

Here are some basic estimates for Poisson point processes that we extensively use in the arguments of this section.

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
\rho\in(0,+\infty)
. For every measurable
F:\mathcal{M}_{\delta}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\to\mathbb{R}
such that
\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\,|F|\,\right]<+\infty
,
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, and finite set
E\subseteq\mathbb{N}_{+}
, we have

(4.9)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[F(\mu_{\rho})\,\mathbf{1}_{\{\mu_{\rho}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}+z)=1\}}\right]

\displaystyle=\rho\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}+z}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[F^{\{1\}}(\mu_{\rho})\,\mathbf{1}_{\{\mu_{\rho}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}+z)=0\}}\right]\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1},

\displaystyle|\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[F(\mu_{\rho})\ \mathbf{1}_{\{\mu_{\rho}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}+z)\geqslant|E|\}}\right]|

\displaystyle\leqslant\rho^{|E|}\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}+z)^{E}}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\bigl[\,|F^{E}(\mu_{\rho})|\,\bigr].

For every measurable function
H:\mathcal{M}_{\delta}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
satisfying the integrability condition

\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\int_{U}|H(\mu_{\rho},x)|\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\rho}(x)\right]<+\infty
,
we have Mecke’s identity (c.f also
[
31
, Chapter 1]
)

(4.10)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\frac{1}{\rho\,|U|}\int_{U}H(\mu_{\rho},x)\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\rho}(x)\right]=\strokedint_{U}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[H(\mu_{\rho}+\delta_{x},x)\right]\,{\mathrm{d}}x.

Proof of Lemma
4.5
.

Without loss of generality, we may fix
z=0
in (
4.9
). The first identity there follows immediately if we spell out the definition of the expectation
\mathbb{E}_{\rho}
and use the independence of increments of the Poisson point process:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[F(\mu_{\rho})\,\mathbf{1}_{\{\mu_{\rho}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}})=1\}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\bigl[e^{-\rho}\rho\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}}F(\delta_{x_{1}}+\mu_{\rho}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.02773pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=4.30554pt}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}})^{c})\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\bigr]

\displaystyle=\rho\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\bigl[\mathbf{1}_{\{\mu_{\rho}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}})=0\}}F(\delta_{x_{1}}+{\mu_{\rho}})\bigr]\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}.

For the second estimate in (
4.9
), we write
n:=|E|
and observe that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[F(\mu_{\rho})\,\mathbf{1}_{\mu_{\rho}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}})\geqslant|E|}\right]=\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[e^{-\rho}\sum_{k=n}^{\infty}\frac{\rho^{k}}{k!}\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}})^{k}}F(\sum_{i=1}^{k}\delta_{x_{i}}+\mu_{\rho}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.02773pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=4.30554pt}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}})^{c})\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\cdots\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{k}\right].

This allows us to bound

\displaystyle|

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[F(\mu_{\rho})\,\mathbf{1}_{\{\mu_{\rho}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}})\geqslant|E|\}}\right]|\leqslant\rho^{n}\,\times

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}^{n}}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\sum_{k=n}^{\infty}e^{-\rho}\frac{\rho^{k-n}}{(k-n)!}\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}})^{k-n}}|F(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\delta_{y_{j}}+\sum_{i=1}^{k-n}\delta_{x_{i}}+\mu_{\rho}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.02773pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=4.30554pt}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}^{c})|\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\cdots\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{k-n}\right]{\mathrm{d}}y_{1}\cdots\,{\mathrm{d}}y_{n}.

which is the second estimate in (
4.9
).
Finally, (
4.10
) may be obtained from the definition of
\mathbb{E}_{\rho}
and the invariance of
H
under permutations of the atoms in
\mu
.
∎

4.1.
Representation formula and corrector estimates

In this subsection we prove Lemmas
4.2
and
4.3
.

Proof of Lemma
4.2
.

We use the definition of
\Delta_{m}^{\rho}(\rho_{0})
and (
3.11
) to write

\displaystyle\Delta^{\rho}_{m}(\rho_{0})=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{(\rho_{0}+\rho)|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}q\cdot\nabla\psi^{\rho}_{m}\,{\mathrm{d}}(\mu+\mu_{\rho})\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}q\cdot\nabla\psi_{m}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right].

Identity (
4.10
) of Lemma
4.5
applied to
\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho}
, first with density
\rho
(with respect to to
\mu_{\rho}
) and then
\rho_{0}
(with respect to
\mu
), yields that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{(\rho_{0}+\rho)|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}q\cdot\nabla\psi^{\rho}_{m}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu_{\rho}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{\rho}{\rho_{0}(\rho_{0}+\rho)|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}q\cdot\nabla\psi^{\rho}_{m}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right],

and, hence, also

(4.11)

\displaystyle\Delta^{\rho}_{m}(\rho_{0})=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}q\cdot(\nabla\psi^{\rho}_{m}-\nabla\psi_{m})\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right].

To establish representation (
4.6
) it now only remains to apply (
3.22
) in Proposition
3.3
and (
3.21
) with the choice
E=F=\emptyset
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
4.3
.

We start by noting that if
E=F=\emptyset
, then the inequalities of Lemma
4.3
correspond to the basic energy estimate in (
3.12
). Hence, we only need to focus on the cases
E,F\neq\emptyset
. With no loss of generality, we prove (
4.7
) with
E=\{1\}
or
E=\{1,2\}
and (
4.8
) with
F=\{1\}
.
We also stress that, since by construction the maximizer
\psi_{m}
is
\mathcal{F}_{Q_{3^{m}+1}}
-measurable, for every non-empty subset
G\subseteq\mathbb{N}
we have that
D_{G}\psi_{m}
vanishes whenever one of the particles
\{x_{j}\}_{j\in G}
does not belong to
Q_{3^{m}+1}
(c.f. Definitions (
2.11
) and (
2.10
)). This implies that in (
4.7
)-(
4.8
) we may replace the integrals over
\mathbb{R}^{d}
by integrals over any set
U\supseteq Q_{3^{m}+1}
. In line with the notation of Section
3
, throughout the proof we fix
U={\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}
.

We start with (
4.7
) when
E=\{1\}
. In view of the previous remarks and spelling out the integrand, this may be rewritten as

(4.12)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}|\nabla(\psi_{m}^{\{1\}}-\psi_{m})|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\lesssim 1,

We consider identity (
3.21
) of Proposition
3.3
with
\rho=0
,
E=\emptyset
and
E=\{1\}
and with test function
D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}
(c.f. Remark
3.5
).

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}(\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}\cdot\mathbf{a}\nabla\psi_{m}-\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}\cdot q)\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]=0,

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}(\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}\cdot\mathbf{a}^{\{1\}}\nabla\psi_{m}^{\{1\}}-\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}\cdot q)\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]=0.

Subtracting the resulting identities yields that

(4.13)

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}\cdot\mathbf{a}^{\{1\}}

\displaystyle\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\bigr]\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}

\displaystyle=-\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}\cdot(D_{\{1\}}\mathbf{a})\,\nabla\psi_{m}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\bigr]\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}.

We now appeal to the uniform ellipticity assumption (
2.1
) and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality to infer from (
4.13
) that

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}

\displaystyle|\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\bigr]\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\lesssim}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}(D_{\{1\}}\mathbf{a})^{2}|\nabla\psi_{m}|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}\mu\bigr]\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}.

We obtain the first inequality in (
4.12
) after noting that (
2.2
) and (
2.1
) for
\mathbf{a}
imply that also

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}}\mathbb{E}

\displaystyle\bigl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}(D_{\{1\}}\mathbf{a})^{2}|\nabla\psi_{m}|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}\mu\bigr]\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathbb{E}\bigl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}+z}|\nabla\psi_{m}(\mu,z)|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu(z)\bigr]\mathrel{{\mathop{\lesssim}\limits}}1.

This establishes (
4.12
).

The proof of (
4.7
) when
E=\{1,2\}
follows a similar argument. Observe that

\displaystyle D_{\{1,2\}}\psi_{m}=\psi_{m}^{\{1,2\}}-\psi_{m}^{\{1\}}-\psi_{m}^{\{2\}}+\psi_{m},

we may add and subtract suitable combinations of identity (
3.21
) in Proposition
3.3
with
E\in\{\emptyset,\{1\},\{2\},\{1,2\}\}
and test function
\nabla D_{\{1,2\}}\psi_{m}
(c.f. Remark
3.5
) to infer that

(4.14)

\displaystyle\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{2}}\hskip-2.84544pt\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla D_{\{1,2\}}\psi_{m}\cdot\mathbf{a}^{\{1,2\}}\nabla D_{\{1,2\}}\psi_{m}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\biggr]\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{2}\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{2}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla D_{\{1,2\}}\psi_{m}\cdot(\mathbf{a}^{\{i\}}-\mathbf{a}^{\{1,2\}})\nabla D_{\{i\}}\psi_{m}{\mathrm{d}}\mu\biggr]{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{2}

\displaystyle-\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{2}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla D_{\{1,2\}}\psi_{m}\cdot(D_{\{1,2\}}\mathbf{a})\,\nabla\psi_{m}{\mathrm{d}}\mu\biggr]{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{2}.

From this, we obtain (
4.7
) when
E=\{1,2\}
as was done in the case
E=\{1\}
. This time, besides the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and (
2.1
)-(
2.2
), we also rely on inequality (
4.12
) for
|E|=1
that was proved above.

To conclude the proof of this lemma, it remains to establish inequality (
4.8
). As argued at the beginning of the proof of this lemma, this can be reduced to

(4.15)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}|\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{\{1\}}}\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}\,|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\lesssim 1.

We appeal again to Proposition
3.3
: we subtract (
3.21
) with
E=\emptyset
and test function
D_{\{2\}}\psi_{m}
(c.f. Remark
3.5
) from the same identity with
E=\{1\}
and test function
D_{\{2\}}\psi_{m}
. This yields

\displaystyle\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{\{1,2\}}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla D_{\{2\}}\psi_{m}\cdot\mathbf{a}\,\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\bigr]

\displaystyle=-\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{\{1,2\}}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla D_{\{2\}}\psi_{m}\cdot(D_{\{1\}}\mathbf{a})\,\nabla\psi_{m}^{\{1\}}{\mathrm{d}}\mu\bigr].

Appealing to Fubini’s theorem and observing that, by a simple relabelling of the integration variable, it holds that
\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{\{1\}}}D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}=\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{\{2\}}}D_{\{2\}}\psi_{m}
, we infer that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\bigl(\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{\{1\}}}D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}\bigr)\cdot\mathbf{a}\,\nabla\bigl(\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{\{1\}}}D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}\bigr)\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\bigr]

\displaystyle=-\mathbb{E}\bigl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\bigl(\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{\{1\}}}D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}\bigr)\cdot\bigl(\int_{/{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{\{1\}}}(D_{\{1\}}\mathbf{a})\,\nabla\psi_{m}^{\{1\}}\bigr)\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\bigr].

By (
2.1
) and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, this also implies that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}|\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{\{1\}}}\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}\,|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\bigr]

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathbb{E}\bigl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}|\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{\{1\}}}(D_{\{1\}}\mathbf{a})\nabla\psi_{m}^{\{1\}}\,|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}\mu\bigr].

We thus conclude the proof of (
4.15
) provided that the term on the right-hand side above is
\lesssim 1
: by the triangle inequality we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\bigl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}|\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{\{1\}}}(D_{\{1\}}\mathbf{a})\nabla\psi_{m}^{\{1\}}\,|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}\mu\bigr]

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathbb{E}\bigl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}|\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{\{1\}}}(D_{\{1\}}\mathbf{a})\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}\,|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}\mu\bigr]

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+\mathbb{E}\bigl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}|\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{\{1\}}}(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{\{1\}})\,|^{2}|\nabla\psi_{m}|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}\mu\bigr].

The second term on the right-hand side is immediately bounded by
\lesssim 1
due to assumptions (
2.1
)-(
2.2
) on
\mathbf{a}
and (
3.12
). The first term admits the same upper bound thanks to the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, (
2.1
)-(
2.2
), and (
4.12
). The proof of Lemma
4.3
is complete.
∎

4.2.
Proof of Proposition
4.1

In this section we use Lemmas
4.2
and
4.3
to show Proposition
4.1
.

Proof of Proposition
4.1
.

Limit (
4.3
) follows immediately from definitions (
4.1
), (
4.2
) and (
2.5
). We thus turn to (
4.4
) and prove this inequality in three different steps.

Step 1.
We claim that

(4.16)

\displaystyle\bigl|

\displaystyle\Delta_{m}^{\rho}(\rho_{0})-\rho c_{1,m}(\rho_{0})\bigr|\lesssim\rho^{2}

\displaystyle+\rho\left|\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi_{m}\cdot\bigl(D_{\{1\}}\mathbf{a}\bigr)(\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho,\{1\}}-\nabla\psi_{m}^{\{1\}}){\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\right|.

We begin by using the representation formula for
\Delta_{m}^{\rho}(\rho_{0})
of Lemma
4.2
, the definition of the expectation
\mathbb{E}
and assumption (
2.2
) for
\mathbf{a}
to rewrite

(4.17)

\displaystyle\Delta_{m}^{\rho}(\rho_{0})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi_{m}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\mu_{\rho}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}+z)\geqslant 1}(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{\rho})\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho}\right]{\mathrm{d}}\mu(z)\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi_{m}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\mu_{\rho}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}+z)=1}(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{\rho})\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho}\right]{\mathrm{d}}\mu(z)\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi_{m}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\mu_{\rho}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}+z)\geqslant 2}(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{\rho})\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho}\right]{\mathrm{d}}\mu(z)\right].

We claim that the second term on the right-hand side above is bounded by a constant multiple of
\rho^{2}
: using the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, the bound (
3.12
), and the second inequality in (
4.9
) of Lemma
4.5
with
E=\{1,2\}
, we infer that

(4.18)

\displaystyle\biggl|\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi_{m}

\displaystyle\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\mu_{\rho}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}+z)\geqslant 2}(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{\rho})\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho}\right]{\mathrm{d}}\mu\biggr]\biggr|

\displaystyle\lesssim\rho^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\bigl(\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}+z)^{2}}|\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho,\{1,2\}}|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{2}\bigr){\mathrm{d}}\mu(z)\right],

and the right-hand side is
\lesssim\rho^{2}
, as one can see by writing

\displaystyle\psi_{m}^{\rho,\{1,2\}}=D_{\{1,2\}}\psi_{m}^{\rho}-D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}^{\rho}-D_{\{2\}}\psi_{m}^{\rho}+\psi_{m}^{\rho},

and then applying the triangle inequality and Lemma
4.3
. Inserting this into (
4.17
), we have that

(4.19)

\displaystyle\left|\Delta_{m}^{\rho}(\rho_{0})-\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi_{m}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\mu_{\rho}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}+z)=1}(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{\rho})\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho}\right]{\mathrm{d}}\mu(z)\right]\right|\lesssim\rho^{2}.

We now apply the first inequality of (
4.9
) to the inner expectation in the term on the left-hand side above. This, together with the locality (
2.2
) of
\mathbf{a}
, yields that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi_{m}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\mu_{\rho}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}+z)=1}(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{\rho})\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho}\right]{\mathrm{d}}\mu(z)\right]

\displaystyle=\rho\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi_{m}\cdot\bigl(\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}+z}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\mu_{\rho}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}+z)=0}(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{\{1\}})\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho,1}\right]\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\bigr){\mathrm{d}}\mu(z)\right]

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\mathop{=}\limits}}\rho\,c_{m,1}-\rho\,\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi_{m}\cdot\bigl(\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}+z}(D_{\{1\}}\mathbf{a})(\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho,\{1\}}-\nabla\psi_{m}^{\{1\}})\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\bigr){\mathrm{d}}\mu(z)\right]

\displaystyle+\rho\,\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi_{m}\cdot\bigl(\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}+z}\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\left[\mathbf{1}_{\mu_{\rho}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}+z)\geqslant 1}(D_{\{1\}}\mathbf{a})\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho,\{1\}}\right]\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\bigr){\mathrm{d}}\mu(z)\right].

To conclude from this and (
4.19
) that inequality (
4.16
) holds, it remains to prove that the last term above is
\lesssim\rho^{2}
. This may be done using again the second inequality in (
4.9
) and Lemma
4.3
, as done for the term in (
4.18
).

Step 2.
We now argue that the term appearing on the right-hand side of (
4.16
) may be rewritten as follows:

(4.20)

\displaystyle

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\nabla\psi_{m}\cdot(D_{\{1\}}\mathbf{a})(\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho,\{1\}}-\nabla\psi_{m}^{\{1\}}){\mathrm{d}}\mu\biggr]\,dx_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}\cdot(\mathbf{a}^{\rho,\{1\}}-\mathbf{a}^{\{1\}})\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho,\{1\}}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\biggr]\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}.

We appeal to Proposition
3.3
: we subtract (
3.21
) with
E=F=\{1\}
from (
3.21
) with
E=\emptyset
and
F=\{1\}
. This, together with the symmetry of
\mathbf{a}
, yields that

\displaystyle-\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi_{m}\cdot(D_{\{1\}}\mathbf{a})\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho,\{1\}}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\bigr]{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho,\{1\}}\cdot\mathbf{a}^{\{1\}}\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\bigr]{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}.

We now subtract this inequality from the same one with
\rho=0
(see also the discussion in Remark
3.5
) and conclude that

(4.21)

\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi_{m}\cdot(D_{\{1\}}\mathbf{a})(\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho,\{1\}}-\nabla\psi_{m}^{\{1\}})\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\bigr]\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\\
=\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}(\nabla\psi_{m}^{\{1\}}-\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho,\{1\}})\cdot\mathbf{a}^{\{1\}}\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\bigr]\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}.

We now treat the term on the right-hand side above in an analogous way. We consider (
3.21
) and (
3.22
) in Proposition
3.3
with
E=\{1\}
and test function
D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}
(this is possible by Remark
3.5
). This yields

(4.22)

\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}\cdot\mathbf{a}^{\{1\}}(\nabla\psi_{m}^{\{1\}}-\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho,\{1\}})\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\bigr]{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\\
=\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}\cdot(\mathbf{a}^{\rho,\{1\}}-\mathbf{a}^{\{1\}})\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho,\{1\}}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\bigr]{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}.

We compare the two displays (
4.21
) and (
4.22
), which give (
4.20
) and thus conclude the proof of Step 2.

Step 3.
In this step, we give an estimate that

(4.23)

\displaystyle\biggl|\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}+z}\nabla\psi_{m}\cdot(D_{\{1\}}\mathbf{a})(\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho,\{1\}}-\nabla\psi_{m}^{\{1\}})

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\mathrm{d}}\mu\biggr]\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\biggr|\lesssim\rho.

This, together with the result (
4.16
) of Step 1, will establish Proposition
4.1
.

Appealing to Step 2, the proof of this step can be reduced to establishing that

(4.24)

\displaystyle\biggl|\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\bigl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}\cdot(\mathbf{a}^{\rho,\{1\}}-\mathbf{a}^{\{1\}})\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho,\{1\}}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\bigr]\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\biggr|\lesssim\rho.

We use the triangle inequality to split

(4.25)

\displaystyle\biggl|\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}\cdot\bigl(\mathbf{a}^{\{1\}}-\mathbf{a}^{\rho,\{1\}}\bigr)\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho,\{1\}}{\mathrm{d}}\mu\biggr]\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\biggr|

\displaystyle\leqslant\biggl|\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}\cdot\bigl(\mathbf{a}^{\{1\}}-\mathbf{a}^{\rho,\{1\}}\bigr)\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}^{\rho}{\mathrm{d}}\mu\biggr]\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\biggr|

\displaystyle+\biggl|\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}\cdot\bigl(\mathbf{a}^{\{1\}}-\mathbf{a}^{\rho,\{1\}}\bigr)\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho}{\mathrm{d}}\mu\biggr]\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\biggr|,

and treat separately the two integrals above.

We begin with the first one and argue similarly to (
4.17
) of Step 1: we use (
2.2
), Lemma
4.9
and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality to control

\displaystyle\biggl|\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}\cdot\bigl(\mathbf{a}^{\{1\}}-\mathbf{a}^{\rho,\{1\}}\bigr)\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}^{\rho}{\mathrm{d}}\mu\biggr]\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\biggr|

\displaystyle\leqslant\rho\biggl(\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}|\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}\mu\biggr]\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\biggr)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\times\biggl(\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}+z}|\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}^{\rho,2}|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x_{2}{\mathrm{d}}\mu(z)\biggr]\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\biggr)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\mathop{\lesssim}\limits}}\rho\,\biggl(\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}+z}|\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}^{\rho,2}|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}x_{2}{\mathrm{d}}\mu(z)\biggr]\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\biggr)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Writing

\displaystyle D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}^{\rho,2}

\displaystyle=\psi_{m}^{\rho,\{1,2\}}-\psi_{m}^{\rho,\{2\}}=D_{\{1,2\}}\psi_{m}^{\rho}-D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}^{\rho},

and appealing again to the triangle inequality and to the estimates of Lemma
4.3
, we infer that

(4.26)

\displaystyle\biggl|\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}\cdot\bigl(\mathbf{a}^{\{1\}}-\mathbf{a}^{\rho,\{1\}}\bigr)\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}^{\rho}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\biggr]\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\biggr|\lesssim\rho.

The second integral in (
4.25
) may be treated in a similar way, if we split

\displaystyle\biggl|\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}}\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}\cdot\bigl(\mathbf{a}^{\{1\}}-\mathbf{a}^{\rho,\{1\}}\bigr)\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho}{\mathrm{d}}\mu\biggr]\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\biggr|

\displaystyle\leqslant\biggl|\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}\setminus({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}+z)}\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\bigl[\bigl(\mathbf{a}^{\{1\}}-\mathbf{a}^{\rho,\{1\}}\bigr)\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho}\bigr]\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\biggr]\biggr|

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\biggl|\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}+z}\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\bigl[\bigl(\mathbf{a}^{\{1\}}-\mathbf{a}^{\rho,\{1\}}\bigr)\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho}\bigr]\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\biggr]\biggr|.

The second term may be bounded by
\lesssim\rho
using again (
2.2
) and an argument analogous to the one used for (
4.26
). On the other hand, since by (
2.2
), for every
z\in{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}
and
x\in{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}\setminus({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}+z)
we have that

\mathbf{a}^{\{1\}}(\mu,z)-\mathbf{a}^{\rho,\{1\}}(\mu,z)=\mathbf{1}_{\{\mu_{\rho}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}+z)\geqslant 1\}}\bigl(\mathbf{a}(\mu,z)-\mathbf{a}^{\rho}(\mu,z)\bigr),

the first term on the right-hand side above may be rewritten as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}\setminus({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}+z)}\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\bigl[\bigl(\mathbf{a}^{\{1\}}-\mathbf{a}^{\rho,\{1\}}\bigr)\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho}\bigr]\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\biggr]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\biggl[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\bigl(\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}\setminus({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}+z)}\nabla D_{\{1\}}\psi_{m}{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\bigr)\cdot\mathbb{E}_{\rho}\bigl[\mathbf{1}_{\{\mu_{\rho}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}+z)\geqslant 1\}}\bigl(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{\rho}\bigr)\nabla\psi_{m}^{\rho}\bigr]{\mathrm{d}}\mu\biggr].

We may bound this term by
\lesssim\rho
by appealing once again to the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and Lemmas
4.3
and
4.9
. This yields that also the second integral in (
4.25
) is bounded by
\lesssim\rho
. This establishes (
4.24
) and concludes the proof of Step 3. Proposition
4.1
is therefore proved.
∎

4.3.
Proof of Theorem
2.1

Equipped with Proposition
4.1
, we are now ready to prove the main result of this section.

Proof of Theorem
2.1
.

A first consequence of Proposition
4.1
is that
\{c_{1,m}(\rho_{0})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
in (
4.5
) is uniformly bounded over
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Indeed, by definition (
4.5
), assumptions (
2.1
)-(
2.2
) on
\mathbf{a}
and the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, we have that

\displaystyle|c_{1,m}(\rho_{0})|

\displaystyle=\biggl|\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi_{m}\cdot\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}+z)}(\mathbf{a}^{\{1\}}-\mathbf{a})\nabla\psi_{m}^{\{1\}}\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu(z)\right]\biggr|

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}|\nabla\psi_{m}|^{2}{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}+z}|\nabla\psi_{m}^{\{1\}}|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}.

The first factor on the right-hand side above is bounded thanks to (
3.12
). The second one can be controlled by the triangle inequality, Lemma
4.3
and again (
3.12
).

Let
\rho_{0}>0
be fixed. The uniform bound for
\{c_{1,m}(\rho_{0})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
implies that we may find a subsequence (possibly depending on
\rho_{0}
) and a number
c^{*}_{1}(\rho_{0})
such that

\lim_{j\to+\infty}c_{1,m_{j}}(\rho_{0}):=c^{*}_{1}(\rho_{0}).

Passing to the limit along this subsequence in the inequality (
4.4
) of Proposition
4.1
and using (
4.3
), we infer that for every
\rho>0

(4.27)

\displaystyle|\Delta^{\rho}(\rho_{0})-c^{*}_{1}(\rho_{0})\rho|\lesssim\rho^{2}.

On the one hand, the arbitrariness of
\rho>0
in this inequality implies that the value
c_{1}^{*}(\rho_{0})
is the limit for the full sequence
\{c_{m,1}(\rho_{0})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
, which we denote by
c_{1}(\rho_{0})
. On the other hand, definition (
4.1
) allows to immediately infer that for every
\rho_{0}>0
fixed and
\rho\geqslant 0
tending to zero, we have

(4.28)

\displaystyle q\cdot{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}^{-1}(\rho_{0}+\rho)q=q\cdot{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}^{-1}(\rho_{0})q+c_{1}(\rho_{0})\rho+O(\rho^{2}).

To conclude the proof of Theorem
2.1
, it thus remains to extend (
4.28
) to negative values of
\rho
that tend to zero. We do this by applying (
4.28
) with the pair
(\rho_{0},\rho_{0}+\rho)
substituted with
(\rho_{0}-\rho,\rho_{0})
to get that, as
\rho\geqslant 0
tends to zero,

(4.29)

\displaystyle q\cdot{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}^{-1}(\rho_{0}-\rho)q=q\cdot{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}^{-1}(\rho_{0})q-c_{1}(\rho_{0}-\rho)\rho+O(\rho^{2}).

To conclude the desired expansion, it remains to show that we may replace
c_{1}(\rho_{0}-\rho)
by
c_{1}(\rho_{0})
in this display. Defining
f(\cdot):=q\cdot{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}^{-1}(\cdot)q
and appealing to identity (
4.28
), we write

\displaystyle c_{1}(\rho_{0}-\rho)

\displaystyle=\frac{f(\rho_{0})-f(\rho_{0}-\rho)}{\rho}+O(\rho)

\displaystyle=\frac{f(\rho_{0})-f(\rho_{0}+\rho)}{\rho}+\frac{f(\rho_{0}+\rho)-f(\rho_{0}-\rho)}{\rho}+O(\rho)

\displaystyle=-c_{1}(\rho_{0})+2c_{1}(\rho_{0}-\rho)+O(\rho).

In the last line, we apply (
4.28
) at
\rho_{0}
for the first term, and (
4.28
) at
(\rho_{0}-\rho)
for the second term wit step size
2\rho
. The notation
O(\rho)
is valid as the hidden constant is independent from the density. The equation above gives us

\displaystyle c_{1}(\rho_{0}-\rho)=c_{1}(\rho_{0})+O(\rho).

Inserting this into (
4.29
) yields that (
4.28
) holds also for negative perturbations
\rho
. This completes the proof of Theorem
2.1
.
∎

5.
Higher-order differentiability

The goal of this section is to generalize the results of the previous section, and ultimately prove Theorem
2.3
stating that the mapping
\rho_{0}\mapsto{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}(\rho_{0})
is infinitely differentiable.

As a preparation, we recall that the notation
f^{E}
and
D_{E}
is introduced in (
2.9
), (
2.11
) and state basic algebraic properties of these operators.

Proposition 5.1

(Algebraic properties)
.

For every
{f,g:\mathcal{M}_{\delta}({\mathbb{R}^{d}})\to\mathbb{R}}
and every finite set
E\subseteq\mathbb{N}_{+}
, the following identities hold.

•

Inclusion-exclusion formula

(5.1)

\displaystyle D_{E}f=\sum_{F\subseteq E}(-1)^{|E\setminus F|}f^{F}.

•

Telescoping formula

(5.2)

\displaystyle f^{E}=\sum_{F\subseteq E}D_{F}f.

•

Leibniz formulas

(5.3)

\displaystyle D_{E}(fg)=\sum_{F\subseteq E}(D_{F}f)(D_{E\setminus F}g^{F}),

and

(5.4)

\displaystyle D_{E}(fg)=\sum_{F,G\subseteq E,F\cup G=E}(D_{F}f)(D_{G}g).

Proof.

These elementary identities can be proved by induction. We show (
5.3
) for illustration. Without loss of generality, we can assume that
E=[\![1,n]\!]
for some integer
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
. The case
n=1
is clear:

(5.5)

\displaystyle D_{1}(fg)=f^{\{1\}}g^{\{1\}}-fg=(D_{1}f)g^{\{1\}}+f(D_{1}g).

Assuming that the formula is valid for
E=[\![1,n]\!]
, we can then write

\displaystyle D_{[\![1,n+1]\!]}(fg)

\displaystyle=D_{n+1}(D_{[\![1,n]\!]}(fg))

\displaystyle=D_{n+1}\left(\sum_{F\subseteq[\![1,n]\!]}(D_{F}f)(D_{[\![1,n]\!]\setminus F}g^{F})\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{F\subseteq[\![1,n]\!]}D_{n+1}\left((D_{F}f)(D_{[\![1,n]\!]\setminus F}g^{F})\right).

We then use (
5.5
) to assert that

\displaystyle D_{n+1}\left((D_{F}f)(D_{[\![1,n]\!]\setminus F}g^{F})\right)

\displaystyle=(D_{F\cup\{n+1\}}f)(D_{[\![1,n]\!]\setminus F}g^{F\cup\{n+1\}})+(D_{F}f)(D_{[\![1,n+1]\!]\setminus F}g^{F})

\displaystyle=(D_{F\cup\{n+1\}}f)(D_{[\![1,n+1]\!]\setminus(F\cup\{n+1\})}g^{F\cup\{n+1\}})+(D_{F}f)(D_{[\![1,n+1]\!]\setminus F}g^{F}).

Combining the two previous displays yields the claim.
∎

5.1.
Main strategy

In this section, we present the structure of the proof of Theorem
2.3
. We will in fact mostly focus on the following finite-volume version of this statement. Recall the definitions of
\Delta^{\rho}
and
\Delta^{\rho}_{m}
in (
4.1
) and (
4.2
), and we know from (
4.1
) that
{\Delta^{\rho}=\lim_{m\to\infty}\Delta^{\rho}_{m}}
. In order to lighten the expressions appearing below, we use the notation in (
3.19
) to simplify the integration with respect to several particles.

Proposition 5.2

(Smoothness in finite volume)
.

For every
\rho_{0}>0
and
k,m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, we define

(5.6)

c_{k,m}(\rho_{0}):=\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{[\![1,k]\!]}}\mathbb{E}\bigg[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi_{m}\cdot D_{[\![1,k]\!]}\left((\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{\#})\nabla\psi^{\#}_{m}\right)\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\bigg].

There exists a positive constant
C_{k}(d,\Lambda)<\infty
such that for every
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
\rho_{0}>0
,

(5.7)

|c_{k,m}(\rho_{0})|\leqslant C_{k}.

Moreover, the quantity
\Delta^{\rho}_{m}(\rho_{0})
defined in (
4.2
) satisfies that, for every
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

(5.8)

\Delta^{\rho}_{m}(\rho_{0})=\sum_{j=1}^{k}c_{j,m}(\rho_{0})\frac{\rho^{j}}{j!}+R_{k}(m,\rho_{0},\rho),

where
R_{k}(m,\rho_{0},\rho)
is such that, as
\rho>0
tends to zero and uniformly over
m
and
\rho_{0}
,
