\displaystyle R_{k}(m,\rho_{0},\rho)=O(\rho^{k+1}).

In Subsection
5.6
, we will obtain our main result Theorem
2.3
as a corollary of Proposition
5.2
. For now, we present the structure of the proof of this proposition.

The first step of the proof of Proposition
5.2
consists in identifying a convenient expansion for
\Delta_{m}^{\rho}
.
As a starting point, one can check that if a function
{f:\mathcal{M}_{\delta}({\mathbb{R}^{d}})\to\mathbb{R}}
is bounded and local, then we have

(5.9)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[f(\mu+\mu_{\rho})]=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{\rho^{k}}{k!}\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{[\![1,k]\!]}}\mathbb{E}[D_{[\![1,k]\!]}f]\right).

(See for instance
[
32
, Theorem 19.2]
; a self-contained argument is given below.) Generalizing this observation, it is natural to expect that
\Delta^{\rho}_{m}
can be rewritten from (
4.6
) as

\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{\rho^{k}}{k!}\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{[\![1,k]\!]}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi_{m}\cdot D_{[\![1,k]\!]}\left((\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{\#})\nabla\psi^{\#}_{m}\right)\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\right),

where we dropped the summand indexed by
k=0
, which vanishes. Notice that in the formula above, we could replace
\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{[\![1,k]\!]}}
with
\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{[\![1,k]\!]}}
, because
\psi_{m}
is
\mathcal{F}_{Q_{3^{m}+1}}
-measurable,
\mathbf{a}
is also local and the perturbation by adding particles outside
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}
will not contribute; this observation will be applied several times in the sequel.
The following lemma states that the expansion formula is indeed valid for
\Delta^{\rho}_{m}
; its proof is provided in Subsection
5.2
.

Lemma 5.3

(Expansion of
\Delta^{\rho}_{m}
)
.

For each
m\in\mathbb{N}
, the quantity
\Delta^{\rho}_{m}
is an analytic function of
\rho
and satisfies

(5.10)

\displaystyle\Delta^{\rho}_{m}=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{\rho^{k}}{k!}\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{[\![1,k]\!]}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi_{m}\cdot D_{[\![1,k]\!]}\left((\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{\#})\nabla\psi^{\#}_{m}\right)\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\right).

The remainder of the proof of Proposition
5.2
consists in the analysis of the summands in the expansion provided Lemma
5.3
.
Applying the Leibniz formula (
5.10
) to these summands:

(5.11)

D_{[\![1,k]\!]}\left((\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{\#})\nabla\psi^{\#}_{m}\right)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}E\cup F=[\![1,k]\!]\end{subarray}}D_{E}(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{\#})(D_{F}\nabla\psi_{m}),

we are led to the expansion

(5.12)

\displaystyle\Delta^{\rho}_{m}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{\rho^{k}}{k!}\sum_{E\cup F=[\![1,k]\!]}I(m,\rho_{0},E,F),

where the quantity
I(m,\rho_{0},E,F)
is defined for
E,F
finite subsets of
\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

(5.13)

I(m,\rho_{0},E,F):=\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{[\![1,k]\!]}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi_{m}\cdot D_{E}(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{\#})D_{F}(\nabla\psi_{m})\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right].

It suffices to give a uniform estimate for the quantity
I(m,\rho_{0},E,F)
with respect to
m
and
\rho_{0}
. Heuristically, the
k
derivatives act either on the conductance or on the corrector, and they compensate with the integration
\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{[\![1,k]\!]}}
. With some more reduction, the estimation of these terms will be based on the following key result.

Proposition 5.4

(Key estimate)
.

There exists a family of constants
\{C(i,j)\}_{i\geqslant j\geqslant 0}
such that for every finite sets
G\subseteq F\subseteq\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
\rho_{0}>0
, we have

(5.14)

\displaystyle\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F\setminus G}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\left|\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G}}D_{F}\nabla\psi_{m}\right|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\leqslant C(|F|,|G|).

The proof of this proposition is based on an induction argument. The base case, for
F=G=\emptyset
, is the standard Dirichlet energy estimate (
3.12
) for
\psi_{m}
. Although this is not necessary, for greater clarity we first present the easier proof of the special case with
G=\emptyset
and arbitrary
F
in Subsection
5.3
. We then give a proof for the general case in Subsection
5.4
. This requires a more careful use of Fubini’s lemma and some inclusion-exclusion argument.
The proof of Proposition
5.2
is then carried out in Subsection
5.5
, by combining the results above according to the outline just discussed.

5.2.
Expansion in finite volume

We prove Lemma
5.3
in this part.

Proof of Lemma
5.3
.

We start by decomposing the expression for
\Delta^{\rho}_{m}
with respect to
\mu_{\rho}\mathbin{\vrule height=6.02773pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=4.30554pt}{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}
, as the particles outside
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}
will not contribute to the perturbation

\displaystyle\Delta^{\rho}_{m}

\displaystyle\mathrel{{\mathop{=}\limits}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi_{m}\cdot(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{\rho})\nabla\psi^{\rho}_{m}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]

\displaystyle=e^{-\rho|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}|}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{(\rho|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}|)^{j}}{j!}\left(\strokedint_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{[\![1,j]\!]}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi_{m}\cdot(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{[\![1,j]\!]})\nabla\psi^{[\![1,j]\!]}_{m}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\right).

We establish first that the series in the above expression converges absolutely. Indeed, using the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and applying the bound (
3.12
) on the Dirichlet energy, we can write

(5.15)

\begin{split}&\left|\strokedint_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{[\![1,j]\!]}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi_{m}\cdot(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{[\![1,j]\!]})\nabla\psi^{[\![1,j]\!]}_{m}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\right|\\
&\leqslant\left(\strokedint_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{[\![1,j]\!]}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}|(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{[\![1,j]\!]})\nabla\psi^{[\![1,j]\!]}_{m}|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
&\qquad\times\left(\strokedint_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{[\![1,j]\!]}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}|\nabla\psi_{m}|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
&\leqslant\left(\strokedint_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{[\![1,j]\!]}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}|(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{[\![1,j]\!]})\nabla\psi^{[\![1,j]\!]}_{m}|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.\end{split}

We introduce the notation

\displaystyle A_{j}:=\strokedint_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{[\![1,j]\!]}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}|(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{[\![1,j]\!]})\nabla\psi^{[\![1,j]\!]}_{m}|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right].

We can further split the integrals contributing to
A_{j}
according to the subset
E\subseteq[\![1,j]\!]
of particles outside of
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}
, leading to

(5.16)

\begin{split}A_{j}&=\sum_{E\subseteq[\![1,j]\!]}\left(\frac{|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}\setminus{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}{|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}|}\right)^{|E|}\left(\frac{|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}{|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}|}\right)^{j-|E|}B_{j,E},\\
B_{j,E}&:=\strokedint_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}\setminus{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})^{E}}\strokedint_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})^{[\![1,j]\!]\setminus E}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}|(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{[\![1,j]\!]})\nabla\psi^{[\![1,j]\!]}_{m}|^{2}\,d\mu\right].\end{split}

Now for (Lebesgue) almost every
(x_{i})_{i\in E}\in({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}\setminus{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})^{E}
fixed, they can be treated together with
\mu\mathbin{\vrule height=6.02773pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=4.30554pt}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})^{c}
as the “outer environment”, and we apply the improved energy inequality (
3.18
) for
(\mu({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})+j-|E|)
particles to obtain that

\displaystyle\strokedint_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})^{[\![1,j]\!]\setminus E}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}|\nabla\psi^{[\![1,j]\!]}_{m}|^{2}\,d\mu\,\biggl|\,\mu({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}),\mu\mathbin{\vrule height=6.02773pt,depth=0.0pt,width=0.55974pt\vrule height=0.55974pt,depth=0.0pt,width=4.30554pt}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})^{c}\right]\leqslant\frac{(\mu({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})+j-|E|)}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}.

From this expression and the uniform ellipticity assumption (
2.1
), one obtains that

\displaystyle B_{j,E}\leqslant C\,\frac{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|+j-|E|}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|},

and thus

\displaystyle A_{j}\leqslant C\sum_{\ell=0}^{j}\binom{j}{\ell}3^{-d(j-\ell)}(1-3^{-d})^{\ell}\left(1+\frac{j-\ell}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\right)\leqslant C\left(1+\frac{j}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\right).

We use this estimate with (
5.15
) to get that

\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{(\rho|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}|)^{j}}{j!}\left|\strokedint_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{[\![1,j]\!]}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi_{m}\cdot(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{[\![1,j]\!]})\nabla\psi^{[\![1,j]\!]}_{m}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\right|\\
\leqslant C\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{(\rho|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}|)^{j}}{j!}\left(1+\frac{j}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}<\infty,

which implies that the series is absolutely convergent. Since
e^{-\rho|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}|}
is analytic with respect to
\rho
, their product
\Delta^{\rho}_{m}
is also an analytic function of
\rho
. Then we expand
e^{-\rho|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}|}
into its Talyor series

\displaystyle\Delta^{\rho}_{m}=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\frac{(-\rho|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}|)^{l}}{l!}

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\frac{(\rho|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}|)^{j}}{j!}

\displaystyle\times\left(\strokedint_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{[\![1,j]\!]}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi_{m}\cdot(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{[\![1,j]\!]})\nabla\psi^{[\![1,j]\!]}_{m}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\right),

and the absolute convergence allows us to reorganize the summations according to

\displaystyle\Delta^{\rho}_{m}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l,j\in\mathbb{N},\\
l+j=k\end{subarray}}^{\infty}

\displaystyle\frac{(-1)^{l}(\rho|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}|)^{k}}{l!j!}

\displaystyle\times\left(\strokedint_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{[\![1,j]\!]}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi_{m}\cdot(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{[\![1,j]\!]})\nabla\psi^{[\![1,j]\!]}_{m}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\right).

We also observe that the part
\strokedint_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{[\![1,j]\!]}}(\cdots)
means the adding of
j
particles in
{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1}
, but the indices do not play a specific role. Thus we have

\strokedint_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{[\![1,j]\!]}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi_{m}\cdot(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{[\![1,j]\!]})\nabla\psi^{[\![1,j]\!]}_{m}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\\
={\binom{k}{j}}^{-1}\sum_{E\subseteq[\![1,k]\!],|E|=j}\strokedint_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{[\![1,k]\!]}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi_{m}\cdot(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{E})\nabla\psi^{E}_{m}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right].

This leads to

\displaystyle\Delta^{\rho}_{m}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}l,j\in\mathbb{N},\\
l+j=k\end{subarray}}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{l}\rho^{k}}{k!}\sum_{E\subseteq[\![1,k]\!],|E|=j}\left(\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{[\![1,k]\!]}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi_{m}\cdot(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{E})\nabla\psi^{E}_{m}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\sum_{E\subseteq[\![1,k]\!]}\frac{(-1)^{k-|E|}\rho^{k}}{k!}\left(\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{[\![1,k]\!]}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi_{m}\cdot(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{E})\nabla\psi^{E}_{m}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{\rho^{k}}{k!}\left(\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{[\![1,k]\!]}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi_{m}\cdot D_{[\![1,k]\!]}((\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{\#})\nabla\psi^{\#}_{m})\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\right).

From the second to the third line, we use the inclusion-exclusion formula (
5.1
). The term
k=0
can be dropped since it vanishes. Finally, we can extend
\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{[\![1,k]\!]}}
to
\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{[\![1,k]\!]}}
and this is the desired result (
5.10
).
∎

5.3.
Key estimate for base case

In this part, for clarity of exposition, we prove (
5.14
) in the simpler case
G=\emptyset
. That is, we show that for every finite
F\subseteq\mathbb{N}_{+}
,

(5.17)

\displaystyle\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\left|D_{F}\nabla\psi_{m}\right|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\leqslant C(|F|,0).

We start by introducing some notation (that will mostly be useful in the more general case treated in the next subsection).
For
x,z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we write
\Upsilon(E,z)
to denote the indicator function

(5.18)

\displaystyle\Upsilon(E,z)(x):=\prod_{i\in E}\mathbf{1}_{\left\{x_{i}\in z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}\right\}}.

We record a handful of elementary observations concerning
\Upsilon
: for every finite sets
E,F\subseteq\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
z\in{\mathbb{R}^{d}}
, we have

(5.19)

\displaystyle\Upsilon(E,z)\Upsilon(F,z)=\Upsilon(E\cup F,z),

(5.20)

\displaystyle\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F}}\Upsilon(E,z)\leqslant\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F\setminus E}}\Upsilon(E\setminus F,z),

and

(5.21)

\displaystyle|D_{E}\mathbf{a}(\mu,z)|\leqslant 2^{|E|}\Lambda\Upsilon(E,z).

Proof of (
5.17
).

The case
F=\emptyset
is the basic energy estimate in (
3.12
), so we now assume that
F\neq\emptyset
. By Proposition
3.3
for
\rho=0
, we have for any finite
E_{1},E_{2}\subseteq\mathbb{N}_{+}
that

(5.22)

\strokedint_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{E_{1}\cup E_{2}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi^{E_{2}}_{m}\cdot\mathbf{a}^{E_{1}}\nabla\psi^{E_{1}}_{m}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\\
=\strokedint_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{E_{1}\cup E_{2}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi^{E_{2}}_{m}\cdot q\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right].

We apply this with
E_{1},E_{2}\subseteq F
, thus we can extend as an average over particles in
F
that

(5.23)

\strokedint_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{F}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi^{E_{2}}_{m}\cdot\mathbf{a}^{E_{1}}\nabla\psi^{E_{1}}_{m}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\\
=\strokedint_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{F}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi^{E_{2}}_{m}\cdot q\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right].

We do the linear combination of (
5.23
) over all the
E_{1}\subseteq F
and apply the inclusion-exclusion formula (
5.1
) to obtain

(5.24)

\displaystyle\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{F}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi^{E_{2}}_{m}\cdot D_{F}\left(\mathbf{a}^{\#}\nabla\psi^{\#}_{m}\right)\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]=0.

Here the sum on the right-hand side is zero thanks to the inclusion-exclusion formula and
F\neq\emptyset
. We extend the integration
\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{F}}
to
\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F}}
and then apply the linear combination of (
5.24
) over all the
E_{2}\subseteq F
to obtain

(5.25)

\displaystyle\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}D_{F}(\nabla\psi_{m})\cdot D_{F}\left(\mathbf{a}^{\#}\nabla\psi^{\#}_{m}\right)\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]=0.

Now we use the Leibniz’s formula in (
5.3
) and obtain that

\displaystyle D_{F}\left(\mathbf{a}^{\#}\nabla\psi^{\#}_{m}\right)=\sum_{G\subseteq F}D_{F\setminus G}(\mathbf{a}^{G})(D_{G}\nabla\psi_{m}).

We put this back to (
5.25
), and keep the term
{(D_{F}\nabla\psi_{m})\cdot\mathbf{a}^{F}(D_{F}\nabla\psi_{m})}
on the left-hand side, while moving the other terms to the right-hand side

\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}(D_{F}\nabla\psi_{m})\cdot\mathbf{a}^{F}(D_{F}\nabla\psi_{m})\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\\
=-\sum_{G\subsetneq F}\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}(D_{F}\nabla\psi_{m})\cdot D_{F\setminus G}(\mathbf{a}^{G})(D_{G}\nabla\psi_{m})\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\right).

Using the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and the triangle inequality, we obtain that

\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}|D_{F}\nabla\psi_{m}|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\leqslant\sum_{G\subsetneq F}\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}|D_{F\setminus G}(\mathbf{a}^{G})|^{2}\left|D_{G}\nabla\psi_{m}\right|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Then we use Fubini’s lemma to pass
\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F\setminus G}}

\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}|D_{F\setminus G}(\mathbf{a}^{G})|^{2}\left|D_{G}\nabla\psi_{m}\right|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\\
=\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F\setminus G}}|D_{F\setminus G}(\mathbf{a}^{G})|^{2}\right)\left|D_{G}\nabla\psi_{m}\right|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right].

The last line uses the fact that
D_{G}\nabla\psi_{m}
does not involve the particle in
F\setminus G
. A varied version of (
5.21
) and (
5.20
) gives us that

\displaystyle\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F\setminus G}}|D_{F\setminus G}(\mathbf{a}^{G})|^{2}\leqslant 4^{|F\setminus G|}\Lambda^{2}\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F\setminus G}}\Upsilon(F\setminus G,\cdot)\leqslant 4^{|F\setminus G|}\Lambda^{2}.

Therefore, we obtain an estimate that

(5.26)

\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}|D_{F}\nabla\psi_{m}|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\leqslant\sum_{G\subsetneq F}\left(4^{|F\setminus G|}\Lambda^{2}\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\left|D_{G}\nabla\psi_{m}\right|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

This estimate allows us to justify the induction argument. Indeed, the case
|F|=0
is the Dirichlet energy estimate. Suppose (
5.17
) is valid for
|F|=n
, then for
|F|=n+1
, we apply (
5.26
). As the quantity on the right-hand side only relies on
G\subsetneq F
, which implies
|G|\leqslant n
, we can invoke (
5.17
) for lower order. This completes the proof of (
5.17
).
∎

5.4.
Key estimate for the general case

In this part, we now treat the general case of (
5.14
).

Proof of Proposition
5.4
.

We decompose the proof into three steps and we suppose
F\neq\emptyset
.

Step 1: Expansion.
We start once again from (
5.23
), and apply a “doubling variables trick”. For
G\subseteq F\subseteq\mathbb{N}_{+}
, we add another set
G^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{N}_{+}\setminus F
such that
|G^{\prime}|=|G|
, and consider (
5.23
) for some
E_{1}\subseteq F
and
E^{\prime}_{2}\subseteq(F\setminus G)\sqcup G^{\prime}
. Then
(E_{1}\cup E^{\prime}_{2})\subseteq(F\sqcup G^{\prime})
and (
5.23
) becomes

\strokedint_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{F\sqcup G^{\prime}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi^{E^{\prime}_{2}}_{m}\cdot\mathbf{a}^{E_{1}}\nabla\psi^{E_{1}}_{m}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\\
=\strokedint_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{F\sqcup G^{\prime}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi^{E^{\prime}_{2}}_{m}\cdot q\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right].

Then we apply the inclusion-exclusion formula (
5.1
) over all
E_{1}\subseteq F
and obtain that

\displaystyle\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{F\sqcup G^{\prime}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi^{E^{\prime}_{2}}_{m}\cdot D_{F}\left(\mathbf{a}^{\#}\nabla\psi^{\#}_{m}\right)\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]=0.

From this line, we can extend
\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m+1})^{F\sqcup G^{\prime}}}
to
\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F\sqcup G^{\prime}}}
. We then apply the inclusion-exclusion formula (
5.1
) over all
E^{\prime}_{2}\subseteq(F\setminus G)\sqcup G^{\prime}
and obtain

\displaystyle\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F\sqcup G^{\prime}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}(D_{(F\setminus G)\sqcup G^{\prime}}\nabla\psi_{m})\cdot D_{F}\left(\mathbf{a}^{\#}\nabla\psi^{\#}_{m}\right)\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]=0.

Notice that the particles in
G^{\prime}
only act on the term
(D_{(F\setminus G)\sqcup G^{\prime}}\nabla\psi_{m})
, we can pass
\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G^{\prime}}}
to the interior and this equation becomes

\displaystyle\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G^{\prime}}}D_{(F\setminus G)\sqcup G^{\prime}}\nabla\psi_{m}\right)\cdot D_{F}\left(\mathbf{a}^{\#}\nabla\psi^{\#}_{m}\right)\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]=0.

Up to a relabelling of the particles, we can write

\displaystyle\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G^{\prime}}}D_{(F\setminus G)\sqcup G^{\prime}}\nabla\psi_{m}=\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G}}D_{F}\nabla\psi_{m},

and obtain a counter-part of (
5.25
) that

\displaystyle\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G}}D_{F}\nabla\psi_{m}\right)\cdot D_{F}\left(\mathbf{a}^{\#}\nabla\psi^{\#}_{m}\right)\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]=0.

Like (
5.25
), we do an expansion for this identity for the term
D_{F}\left(\mathbf{a}^{\#}\nabla\psi^{\#}_{m}\right)
, but we need to treat it more carefully. We apply (
5.4
) on
{D_{F}\left(\mathbf{a}^{\#}\nabla\psi^{\#}_{m}\right)}
and obtain that

\displaystyle D_{F}\left(\mathbf{a}^{\#}\nabla\psi^{\#}_{m}\right)=\sum_{F_{1}\cup F_{2}=F}D_{F_{2}}(\mathbf{a})(D_{F_{1}}\nabla\psi_{m}).

We keep the term

\displaystyle\{F_{1}\cup F_{2}=F\}\cap\{F_{2}\subseteq(F\setminus G)\}\cap\{F_{1}=F\},

on the left-hand side, while putting the other terms

\displaystyle\{F_{1}\cup F_{2}=F\}\cap\left(\{F_{2}\cap G\neq\emptyset\}\cup\{F_{1}\subsetneq F\}\right),

on the right-hand side. We also notice (
5.2
) that

\displaystyle\sum_{F_{2}\subseteq(F\setminus G)}D_{F_{2}}(\mathbf{a})=\mathbf{a}^{F\setminus G},

so we obtain that

(5.27)

\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G}}D_{F}\nabla\psi_{m}\right)\cdot\mathbf{a}^{F\setminus G}(D_{F}\nabla\psi_{m})\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\\
=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}F_{1}\cup F_{2}=F\\
F_{2}\cap G\neq\emptyset,\text{ or }F_{1}\subsetneq F\end{subarray}}-\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G}}D_{F}\nabla\psi_{m}\right)\cdot D_{F_{2}}(\mathbf{a})(D_{F_{1}}\nabla\psi_{m})\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\right).

Because
\mathbf{a}^{F\setminus G},\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G}}D_{F}\psi_{m}
and
{\mathrm{d}}\mu
do not depend on the particles indexed by
G
, we can apply Fubini’s lemma to pass
\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G}}
to interior, thus the left-hand side of (
5.27
)
becomes

\displaystyle\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G}}D_{F}\nabla\psi_{m}\right)\cdot\mathbf{a}^{F\setminus G}(D_{F}\nabla\psi_{m})\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F\setminus G}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G}}D_{F}\nabla\psi_{m}\right)\cdot\mathbf{a}^{F\setminus G}\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G}}D_{F}\nabla\psi_{m}\right)\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]

\displaystyle\geqslant\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F\setminus G}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\left|\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G}}D_{F}\nabla\psi_{m}\right|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right].

For the right-hand side, we argue similarly by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and the triangle inequality to obtain that

(5.28)

\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F\setminus G}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\left|\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G}}D_{F}\nabla\psi_{m}\right|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\leqslant\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}F_{1}\cup F_{2}=F\\
F_{2}\cap G\neq\emptyset,\text{ or }F_{1}\subsetneq F\end{subarray}}\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F\setminus G}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\left|\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G}}D_{F_{2}}(\mathbf{a})(D_{F_{1}}\nabla\psi_{m})\right|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Step 2: Simplification and recurrence inequality.
The final goal is to get a recurrence like (
5.26
), but (
5.28
) still needs some further simplification. We focus on the term

\displaystyle\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G}}D_{F_{2}}(\mathbf{a})(D_{F_{1}}\nabla\psi_{m}).

Since
F_{1}\cup F_{2}=F
, we use the disjoint decomposition that

\displaystyle F=(F_{2}\setminus F_{1})\sqcup(F_{1}\setminus F_{2})\sqcup(F_{2}\cap F_{1}),

which also induces the decomposition of
G

\displaystyle G=((G\cap F_{2})\setminus F_{1})\sqcup((G\cap F_{1})\setminus F_{2})\sqcup(G\cap F_{2}\cap F_{1}).

Thus, we can decompose

\displaystyle\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G}}=\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(G\cap F_{2})\setminus F_{1}}}\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(G\cap F_{1})\setminus F_{2}}}\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G\cap F_{2}\cap F_{1}}},

and pass them respectively to the proper term

\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G}}D_{F_{2}}(\mathbf{a})(D_{F_{1}}\nabla\psi_{m})\\
=\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G\cap F_{2}\cap F_{1}}}\left(\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(G\cap F_{2})\setminus F_{1}}}D_{F_{2}}(\mathbf{a})\right)\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(G\cap F_{1})\setminus F_{2}}}D_{F_{1}}\nabla\psi_{m}\right)\right).

Let
z\in\supp(\mu)
be the particle at which the gradient is computed, then we use the notation (
5.18
) and the estimate (
5.21
) to give its bound

\displaystyle\left|\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G}}D_{F_{2}}(\mathbf{a})D_{F_{1}}\nabla\psi_{m}\right|^{2}(z)

\displaystyle\leqslant\left|\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G\cap F_{2}\cap F_{1}}}\left(\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(G\cap F_{2})\setminus F_{1}}}|D_{F_{2}}(\mathbf{a})|\right)\left|\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(G\cap F_{1})\setminus F_{2}}}D_{F_{1}}\nabla\psi_{m}\right|\right)\right|^{2}(z)

\displaystyle\leqslant 4^{|F_{2}|}\Lambda^{2}\left|\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G\cap F_{2}\cap F_{1}}}\left(\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(G\cap F_{2})\setminus F_{1}}}\Upsilon(F_{2},z)\right)\left|\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(G\cap F_{1})\setminus F_{2}}}D_{F_{1}}\nabla\psi_{m}\right|\right)\right|^{2}(z).

Next, we use the property that
\Upsilon(F_{2},z)
requires all the particles in
F_{2}
to live in
z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}

\displaystyle\left|\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G\cap F_{2}\cap F_{1}}}\left(\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(G\cap F_{2})\setminus F_{1}}}\Upsilon(F_{2},z)\right)\left|\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(G\cap F_{1})\setminus F_{2}}}D_{F_{1}}\nabla\psi_{m}\right|\right)\right|^{2}(z)

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{(z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}})^{G\cap F_{2}\cap F_{1}}}\left(\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(G\cap F_{2})\setminus F_{1}}}\Upsilon(F_{2},\cdot)\right)\left|\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(G\cap F_{1})\setminus F_{2}}}D_{F_{1}}\nabla\psi_{m}\right|\right)\right|^{2}(z)

\displaystyle\leqslant\int_{(z+{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}})^{G\cap F_{2}\cap F_{1}}}\left(\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(G\cap F_{2})\setminus F_{1}}}\Upsilon(F_{2},\cdot)\right)^{2}\left|\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(G\cap F_{1})\setminus F_{2}}}D_{F_{1}}\nabla\psi_{m}\right|^{2}\right)(z)

\displaystyle=\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G\cap F_{2}\cap F_{1}}}\left(\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(G\cap F_{2})\setminus F_{1}}}\Upsilon(F_{2},\cdot)\right)^{2}\left|\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(G\cap F_{1})\setminus F_{2}}}D_{F_{1}}\nabla\psi_{m}\right|^{2}\right)(z).

From the second line to the third line, we use the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, and from the third line to the fourth line, we reapply the property of
\Upsilon(F_{2},z)
. So in this step we gain a small factor for Cauchy–Schwarz inequality. Now, we use the property (
5.20
)

\displaystyle\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(G\cap F_{2})\setminus F_{1}}}\Upsilon(F_{2},z)\leqslant\Upsilon(F_{2}\setminus((G\cap F_{2})\setminus F_{1}),z).

We put all these estimates back to the right-hand side of (
5.28
) to obtain that

(5.29)

\begin{split}&\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F\setminus G}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\left|\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G}}D_{F_{2}}(\mathbf{a})(D_{F_{1}}\nabla\psi_{m})\right|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\\
&\leqslant 4^{|F_{2}|}\Lambda^{2}\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F\setminus G}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\right.\\
&\qquad\left.\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G\cap F_{2}\cap F_{1}}}\left(\Upsilon(F_{2}\setminus((G\cap F_{2})\setminus F_{1}),\cdot)\left|\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(G\cap F_{1})\setminus F_{2}}}D_{F_{1}}\nabla\psi_{m}\right|^{2}\right)\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right].\end{split}

In this integral, we can continue some simplification with
\Upsilon(F_{2}\setminus((G\cap F_{2})\setminus F_{1}),\cdot)
. We have the following disjoint union

F\setminus G=((F_{2}\cap F_{1})\setminus G)\sqcup((F_{2}\setminus F_{1})\setminus G)\sqcup((F_{1}\setminus F_{2})\setminus G),

which implies that

(5.30)

\displaystyle\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F\setminus G}}=\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(F_{2}\cap F_{1})\setminus G}}\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(F_{2}\setminus F_{1})\setminus G}}\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(F_{1}\setminus F_{2})\setminus G}}.

Because
\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(G\cap F_{1})\setminus F_{2}}}D_{F_{1}}\nabla\psi_{m}
only involves a subset of particles in
F_{1}
, which is disjoint from
(F_{2}\setminus F_{1})\setminus G
, we use Fubini’s lemma to pass the integration of
\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(F_{2}\setminus F_{1})\setminus G}}
to the inside

\displaystyle\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(F_{2}\setminus F_{1})\setminus G}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G\cap F_{2}\cap F_{1}}}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left.\left(\Upsilon(F_{2}\setminus((G\cap F_{2})\setminus F_{1}),\cdot)\left|\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(G\cap F_{1})\setminus F_{2}}}D_{F_{1}}\nabla\psi_{m}\right|^{2}\right)\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G\cap F_{2}\cap F_{1}}}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\left.\left(\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(F_{2}\setminus F_{1})\setminus G}}\Upsilon(F_{2}\setminus((G\cap F_{2})\setminus F_{1}),\cdot)\right)\left|\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(G\cap F_{1})\setminus F_{2}}}D_{F_{1}}\nabla\psi_{m}\right|^{2}\right)\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G\cap F_{2}\cap F_{1}}}\left(\Upsilon(F_{2}\cap F_{1},\cdot)\left|\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(G\cap F_{1})\setminus F_{2}}}D_{F_{1}}\nabla\psi_{m}\right|^{2}\right)\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G\cap F_{2}\cap F_{1}}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\left(\Upsilon(F_{2}\cap F_{1},\cdot)\left|\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(G\cap F_{1})\setminus F_{2}}}D_{F_{1}}\nabla\psi_{m}\right|^{2}\right)\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right].

From the second line to the third line, we used (
5.20
) and the decomposition

F_{2}\setminus((G\cap F_{2})\setminus F_{1})=(F_{2}\cap F_{1})\sqcup((F_{2}\setminus F_{1})\setminus G).

See the Venn diagram in Figure
1
to help check this equation. From the third line to the fourth line, we put the integral
\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G\cap F_{2}\cap F_{1}}}
outside the expectation using Fubini’s lemma. We combine this integral together with the rest of integrals in (
5.30
) and we observe that

(5.31)

\displaystyle\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(F_{2}\cap F_{1})\setminus G}}\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(F_{1}\setminus F_{2})\setminus G}}\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G\cap F_{2}\cap F_{1}}}=\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F_{1}\setminus((G\cap F_{1})\setminus F_{2})}}.

because of the identity (see Figure
1
to help check this equation)

\displaystyle((F_{2}\cap F_{1})\setminus G)\sqcup((F_{1}\setminus F_{2})\setminus G)\sqcup(G\cap F_{2}\cap F_{1})=F_{1}\setminus((G\cap F_{1})\setminus F_{2}).

Figure 1.
A Venn diagram for illustration. The disk on the left represents
F_{1}
and the disk on the right represents
F_{2}
. The rectangle is
G
. We use different colors for the partition of
F=F_{1}\cup F_{2}
, and it has the following bijections.

\displaystyle F_{1}\cap F_{2}

\displaystyle=\{\text{yellow, purple}\},

\displaystyle G\cap F_{1}\cap F_{2}

\displaystyle=\{\text{yellow}\},

\displaystyle(F_{1}\cap F_{2})\setminus G

\displaystyle=\{\text{purple}\},

\displaystyle(F_{1}\setminus F_{2})\setminus G

\displaystyle=\{\text{red}\},

\displaystyle(F_{2}\setminus F_{1})\setminus G

\displaystyle=\{\text{blue}\},

\displaystyle F_{1}\setminus((G\cap F_{1})\setminus F_{2})

\displaystyle=\{\text{red, yellow, purple}\},

\displaystyle F_{2}\setminus((G\cap F_{2})\setminus F_{1})

\displaystyle=\{\text{blue, yellow, purple}\}.

Therefore, one term in the right-hand side of (
5.28
) can be bounded

\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F\setminus G}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\left|\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{G}}D_{F_{2}}(\mathbf{a})(D_{F_{1}}\nabla\psi_{m})\right|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\\
\leqslant\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F_{1}\setminus((G\cap F_{1})\setminus F_{2})}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\left(\Upsilon(F_{2}\cap F_{1},\cdot)\left|\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(G\cap F_{1})\setminus F_{2}}}D_{F_{1}}\nabla\psi_{m}\right|^{2}\right)\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right].

We can further drop out the indicator
\Upsilon(F_{2}\cap F_{1},\cdot)
, and put it back to (
5.28
) to obtain that

(5.32)

\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F\setminus G}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\left|\int_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})^{G}}(D_{F}\nabla\psi_{m})\right|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\leqslant\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}F_{1}\cup F_{2}=F\\
F_{2}\cap G\neq\emptyset,\text{ or }F_{1}\subsetneq F\end{subarray}}\left(4^{|F_{2}|}\Lambda^{2}\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F_{1}\setminus((G\cap F_{1})\setminus F_{2})}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\left|\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{(G\cap F_{1})\setminus F_{2}}}D_{F_{1}}\nabla\psi_{m}\right|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Step 3: Induction argument.
Equation (
5.32
) is the analogue of (
5.26
) for the general case. In this step, we describe the induction argument, which consists in obtaining a bound for the constant
C(i,j)
in (
5.14
) in terms of a linear combination of the
C(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
with
i^{\prime}\leqslant i
,
j^{\prime}\leqslant j
, and
i^{\prime}+j^{\prime}<i+j
. An illustration is in Figure
2
.

We denote by
\widetilde{I}(m,\rho_{0},F,G)
the left-hand side of (
5.32
). This equation can be rewritten as

(5.33)

\displaystyle\widetilde{I}(m,\rho_{0},F,G)\leqslant\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}F_{1}\cup F_{2}=F\\
F_{2}\cap G\neq\emptyset,\text{ or }F_{1}\subsetneq F\end{subarray}}2^{|F_{2}|}\Lambda\,\widetilde{I}(m,\rho_{0},F_{1},(G\cap F_{1})\setminus F_{2}).

For sets
F_{1},F_{2}
as in the summands above, we clearly have

|F_{1}|+|(G\cap F_{1})\setminus F_{2}|\leqslant|F|+|G|.

In fact, the inequality is always strict. Indeed, a possible case of equality would require that
F_{1}=F
, since
F_{1}\subseteq F
and
(G\cap F_{1})\setminus F_{2}\subseteq G
. But if
F_{1}=F
, then we must have
F_{2}\cap G\neq\emptyset
, and thus

|(G\cap F_{1})\setminus F_{2}|=|G\setminus F_{2}|\leqslant|G|-1.

So all the summands on the right side of (
5.33
) are such that

|F_{1}|+|(G\cap F_{1})\setminus F_{2}|<|F|+|G|.

The induction argument is then clear: the case when
F=G=\emptyset
is the basic Dirichlet energy estimate. Next, assuming the boundedness of
\widetilde{I}(m,\rho_{0},F,G)
for
|F|+|G|\leqslant k
, we can obtain the result for
|F|+|G|=k+1
by an application of (
5.33
).
This completes the proof of Proposition
5.4
.
∎

Figure 2.
An illustration of the recurrence argument. The constant
C(i,j)
can be bounded by a linear combination of the
C(i^{\prime},j^{\prime})
with
j^{\prime}\leqslant j
,
i^{\prime}\leqslant i
, and
i^{\prime}+j^{\prime}<i+j
.

5.5.
Smoothness in finite volume

We can now combine Lemma
5.3
and Proposition
5.4
to complete the proof of Proposition
5.2
.

Proof of Proposition
5.2
.

We decompose the proof into three steps.

Step 1: Decomposition and expansion.

As stated in Subsection
5.1
, we first expand
\Delta^{\rho}_{m}
with respect to
\rho
as in (
5.10
) and use the Leibniz formula (
5.4
) to get that

(5.34)

\displaystyle\Delta^{\rho}_{m}(\rho_{0})

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{\rho^{k}}{k!}c_{k,m}(\rho_{0})=\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{\rho^{k}}{k!}\sum_{E\cup F=[\![1,k]\!]}I(m,\rho_{0},E,F),

with
c_{k,m}
defined in (
5.6
) and
I(m,\rho_{0},E,F)
defined in (
5.13
). Lemma
5.3
ensures that this series converges, and that
\rho\mapsto\Delta^{\rho}_{m}
is analytic. In the next step, we aim to give a bound to
I(m,\rho_{0},E,F)
which is uniform with respect to
m
and
\rho_{0}
.

Step 2: Reduction of
I(m,\rho_{0},E,F)
.
Recall the expression of
I(m,\rho_{0},E,F)
in (
5.13
), we use Fubini’s lemma and pass the integration
[\![1,k]\!]\setminus E
inside. Notice that we have
[\![1,k]\!]\setminus E=F\setminus E
thanks to
E\cup F=[\![1,k]\!]
. Since the particles in the set
F\setminus E
do not appear in
D_{E}(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{\#})
, we have

\displaystyle I(m,\rho_{0},E,F)=\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{E}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\nabla\psi_{m}\cdot D_{E}(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{\#})\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F\setminus E}}D_{F}\nabla\psi_{m}\right)\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right].

We apply the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and obtain that

\displaystyle|I(m,\rho_{0},E,F)|

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{E}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}|D_{E}(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{\#})||\nabla\psi_{m}|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{E}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}|D_{E}(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{\#})|\left|\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F\setminus E}}D_{F}\nabla\psi_{m}\right|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

The first term is easy to treat since we can use Fubini’s lemma that

\displaystyle\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{E}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}|D_{E}(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{\#})||\nabla\psi_{m}|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{E}}|D_{E}(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{\#})|\right)|\nabla\psi_{m}|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant 2^{|E|}\Lambda.

In the last step, we apply (
5.21
) and (
5.20
) that

\displaystyle\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{E}}|D_{E}(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{\#})|\leqslant 2^{|E|}\Lambda\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{E}}\Upsilon(E,\cdot)\leqslant 2^{|E|}\Lambda.

For the second term, we use the decomposition

\displaystyle D_{E}=D_{E\setminus F}\circ D_{E\cap F},\qquad\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{E}}=\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{E\setminus F}}\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{E\cap F}},

and pass the integration
\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{E\setminus F}}
inside

\displaystyle\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{E}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}|D_{E}(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{\#})|\left|\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F\setminus E}}D_{F}\nabla\psi_{m}\right|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]

\displaystyle=\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{E\cap F}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\left(\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{E\setminus F}}|D_{E}(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{\#})|\right)\left|\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F\setminus E}}D_{F}\nabla\psi_{m}\right|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right].

We apply once again the estimate (
5.20
) and (
5.21
) that

\displaystyle\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{E\setminus F}}|D_{E}(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{\#})|\leqslant 2^{|E|}\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{E\setminus F}}\Upsilon(E,\cdot)\leqslant 2^{|E|}\Lambda\Upsilon(E\cap F,\cdot)\leqslant 2^{|E|}\Lambda.

Therefore, we bound the second term by

\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{E}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}|D_{E}(\mathbf{a}-\mathbf{a}^{\#})|\left|\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F\setminus E}}D_{F}\nabla\psi_{m}\right|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\\
\leqslant 2^{|E|}\Lambda\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{E\cap F}}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}\left|\int_{({\mathbb{R}^{d}})^{F\setminus E}}D_{F}\nabla\psi_{m}\right|^{2}\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right].

Here we apply the key estimate Proposition
5.4
to conclude the proof a the uniform bound of
I(m,\rho_{0},E,F)
with respect to
m
and
\rho_{0}
. This also implies the uniform bound (
5.7
) for
c_{k,m}(\rho_{0})
.

Step 3: Control of the tail
R_{k}
.
In this step, we need to control the tail in the expansion of
\Delta^{\rho}_{m}(\rho_{0})
.
Even if one were to keep track of the dependence on
k
in the upper bound
|c_{k,m}(\rho_{0})|\leqslant C_{k}
obtained above, one cannot ensure the summability of the series
\sum_{j>k}\frac{C_{j}}{j!}\rho^{j}
. On the other hand, we know the function
\rho\mapsto\Delta^{\rho}_{m}(\rho_{0})
is indeed analytic for any fixed
\rho_{0}\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
(using a naive bound of
c_{k,m}
depending on
m
, see Lemma
5.3
and its proof in Subsection
5.2
), so for
c_{j,m}
defined in Proposition
5.2
and
\rho_{0}>0
, we have

\displaystyle c_{j,m}(\rho_{0})=\left(\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}\rho}\right)^{j}_{|\rho=0}\Delta^{\rho}_{m}(\rho_{0}).

We also write
\partial^{j}\Delta^{\rho}_{m}
as a shorthand notation for the
j
-th derivative at
\rho
,

\displaystyle\partial^{j}\Delta^{\rho}_{m}(\rho_{0}):=\left(\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}\rho}\right)^{j}\Delta^{\rho}_{m}(\rho_{0}).

Then we apply Taylor’s expansion for the function
\rho\mapsto\Delta^{\rho}_{m}(\rho_{0})
until order
k

(5.35)

\displaystyle\Delta^{\rho}_{m}(\rho_{0})=\sum_{j=0}^{k}\frac{\partial^{j}\Delta^{0}_{m}(\rho_{0})}{j!}\rho^{j}+\int_{0}^{\rho}\frac{\partial^{k+1}\Delta^{s}_{m}(\rho_{0})}{k!}s^{k}\,{\mathrm{d}}s.

Recalling the definition of
\Delta^{\rho}_{m}
in (
4.2
), we have

\displaystyle\partial^{k+1}\Delta^{s}_{m}(\rho_{0})

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}\rho}\right)^{j}_{|\rho=s}\left(q\cdot{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}_{*}^{-1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{0}+\rho)q-q\cdot{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}_{*}^{-1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{0})q\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{{\mathrm{d}}}{{\mathrm{d}}\rho}\right)^{j}_{|\rho=0}\left(q\cdot{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}_{*}^{-1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{0}+s+\rho)q-q\cdot{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}_{*}^{-1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{0}+s)q\right)

\displaystyle=\partial^{k+1}\Delta^{0}_{m}(\rho_{0}+s).

Since
\partial^{k+1}\Delta^{0}_{m}(\rho_{0}+s)=c_{k+1,m}(\rho_{0}+s)
, upon inserting this back into (
5.35
), it follows that

(5.36)

\displaystyle\Delta^{\rho}_{m}(\rho_{0})=\sum_{j=0}^{k}\frac{c_{j,m}(\rho_{0})}{j!}\rho^{j}+\int_{0}^{\rho}\frac{c_{k+1,m}(\rho_{0}+s)}{k!}s^{k}\,{\mathrm{d}}s.

This gives us an expression for the remainder of order
k
in (
5.8
), which is

(5.37)

\displaystyle R_{k}(m,\rho_{0},\rho):=\int_{0}^{\rho}\frac{c_{k+1,m}(\rho_{0}+s)}{k!}s^{k}\,{\mathrm{d}}s.

Using the uniform estimate (
5.7
) of
c_{k+1,m}(\rho_{0}+s)
with respect to
\rho_{0}+s
and
m
, the remainder is of order
O(\rho^{k+1})
independent of
\rho_{0}
and
m
. This finishes our proof of Proposition
5.2
.
∎

5.6.
Proof of the main theorem

In this final subsection, we conclude the proof of the main Theorem
2.3
, using Proposition
5.2
.

Proof of Theorem
2.3
.

As a first step, we show the existence of the limit in (
2.12
). Let
k\geqslant 2
and assume by induction that the existence of
c_{j}(\rho_{0})
is established for
1\leqslant j\leqslant k-1
and
\rho_{0}>0
(recall that the existence of
c_{1}(\rho_{0})
follows from Theorem
2.1
). For
\rho_{0}>0
, the sequence
\{c_{k,m}(\rho_{0})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
defined in (
5.6
) is bounded by some positive constant
C_{k}(d,\Lambda)
using Proposition
5.2
. Thus, there exists a subsequence
\{c_{k,m_{\ell}}(\rho_{0})\}_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}
(possibly depending on
\rho_{0}
) such that

c^{*}_{k}(\rho_{0}):=\lim_{\ell\to+\infty}c_{k,m_{\ell}}(\rho_{0})

exists. By (
5.8
), one has for
\rho>0

\left|\Delta^{\rho}_{m_{\ell}}(\rho_{0})-\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\frac{c_{j,m_{\ell}}(\rho_{0})\rho^{j}}{j!}-\frac{c_{k,m_{\ell}}(\rho_{0})}{k!}\rho^{k}\right|\leqslant|R_{k}(m_{\ell},\rho_{0},\rho)|,

and passing to the limit
\ell\rightarrow\infty
yields (upon using Proposition
5.2
, the induction hypothesis and (
4.3
))

(5.38)

\sup_{\rho_{0}\in(0,\infty)}\left|\Delta^{\rho}(\rho_{0})-\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\frac{c_{j}(\rho_{0})\rho^{j}}{j!}-\frac{c_{k}^{\ast}(\rho_{0})}{k!}\rho^{k}\right|\leqslant O(\rho^{k+1}),\qquad\text{for }\rho>0.

In particular, (
5.38
) implies that
c^{\ast}_{k}(\rho_{0})
is the unique limit of the full sequence
\{c_{k,m}(\rho_{0})\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
, and we denote it by
c_{k}(\rho_{0})
. This proves (
2.12
). We note in passing that

(5.39)
