c_{1},\cdots,c_{k}:(0,\infty)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
are bounded functions,

which follows by (
2.12
) and
|c_{k,m}|\leqslant C_{k}(d,\Lambda)
, see Proposition
5.2
.
Thus, we can write

(5.40)

\Delta^{\rho}(\rho_{0})=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\frac{c_{j}(\rho_{0})}{j!}\rho^{j}+O(\rho^{k+1}),\qquad\text{for }\rho>0,

with the error term independent of
\rho_{0}
. To simplify notation, we again set
f(\cdot):=q\cdot{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}^{-1}(\cdot)q
. We claim that the expansion (
5.40
) implies that

(5.41)

c_{1},\cdots,c_{k}:(0,\infty)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
are Lipschitz-continuous,

and moreover

(5.42)

\text{$f$ has $k$ derivatives, and }f^{(j)}(\rho_{0})=c_{j}(\rho_{0}),\ 1\leqslant j\leqslant k,\ \rho_{0}>0.

We first define the forward difference of order
\ell
of
f
at
\rho_{0}>0
,
\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, with step size
\rho\geqslant 0
as

(5.43)

\Delta^{\ell,\rho}[f](\rho_{0})=\Delta^{\ell,\rho}(\rho_{0}):=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell}\binom{\ell}{i}(-1)^{\ell-i}f(\rho_{0}+i\rho),

(note that with our fixed choice of
f
,
\Delta^{1,\rho}(\rho_{0})=\Delta^{\rho}(\rho_{0})
). We claim that these quantities fulfill for
1\leqslant\ell\leqslant k
,
\rho_{0}>0
and
\rho\geqslant 0
,

(5.44)

\Delta^{\ell,\rho}(\rho_{0})=c_{\ell}(\rho_{0})\rho^{\ell}+O(\rho^{\ell+1}),

with the error term independent of
\rho_{0}
, and for
1\leqslant\ell<k
,
\rho_{0}>0
and
\rho\geqslant 0
,

(5.45)

\Delta^{\ell,\rho}(\rho_{0}+\rho)-\Delta^{\ell,\rho}(\rho_{0})=\Delta^{\ell+1,\rho}(\rho_{0}).

We prove (
5.44
). To this end, we infer from (
5.40
) that

(5.46)

f(\rho_{0}+i\rho)=\sum_{j=0}^{k}\frac{c_{j}(\rho_{0})}{j!}(i\rho)^{j}+O(\rho^{k+1}),\qquad\text{for }\rho>0,1\leqslant i\leqslant k,

where we defined for convenience
c_{0}(\rho_{0}):=f(\rho_{0})
. Equation (
5.46
) is now inserted into (
5.43
), which yields for
\rho_{0}>0
,
\rho\geqslant 0
and
1\leqslant\ell\leqslant k
that

\displaystyle\Delta^{\ell,\rho}(\rho_{0})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell}\binom{\ell}{i}(-1)^{\ell-i}\left\{\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}\frac{c_{j}(\rho_{0})}{j!}(i\rho)^{j}+\sum_{j=\ell+1}^{k}\frac{c_{j}(\rho_{0})}{j!}(i\rho)^{j}+O(\rho^{k+1})\right\}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{\ell}\frac{c_{j}(\rho_{0})}{j!}\rho^{j}\left(\sum_{i=0}^{\ell}\binom{\ell}{i}(-1)^{\ell-i}i^{j}\right)+O(\rho^{\ell+1})

\displaystyle=c_{\ell}(\rho_{0})\rho^{\ell}+O(\rho^{\ell+1}).

Here, we combined the terms involving
\rho^{j}
with
j\in\{\ell+1,\cdots,k\}
and
O(\rho^{k+1})
into a contribution
O(\rho^{\ell+1})
and using (
5.39
) in going from the first to the second line. From the second to the third line, we use the fact that for any polynomial
P
with real coefficients
A_{0},\cdots,A_{k}
, i.e.,
{P(X)=A_{\ell}X^{\ell}+\cdots+A_{0}}
of degree smaller or equal to
\ell
, one has
{\sum_{i=0}^{\ell}\binom{\ell}{i}(-1)^{\ell-i}P(i)=\ell!A_{\ell}}
. Equation (
5.45
) also follows directly from elementary properties of binomial coefficients. Indeed:

\displaystyle\Delta^{\ell,\rho}(\rho_{0}+\rho)-\Delta^{\ell,\rho}(\rho_{0})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell}(-1)^{\ell-i}\binom{\ell}{i}f(\rho_{0}+(i+1)\rho)-\sum_{i=0}^{\ell}(-1)^{\ell-i}\binom{\ell}{i}f(\rho_{0}+i\rho)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell+1}(-1)^{\ell-i+1}\binom{\ell}{i-1}f(\rho_{0}+i\rho)+\sum_{i=0}^{\ell}(-1)^{\ell-i+1}\binom{\ell}{i}f(\rho_{0}+i\rho)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell+1}(-1)^{\ell+1-i}\left\{\binom{\ell}{i-1}+\binom{\ell}{i}\right\}f(\rho_{0}+i\rho)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell+1}(-1)^{\ell+1-i}\binom{\ell+1}{i}f(\rho_{0}+i\rho)=\Delta^{\ell+1,\rho}(\rho_{0}).

Identity (
5.44
) is now used at
\rho_{0}+\rho
and
\rho_{0}
on the left-hand side of (
5.45
), and at
\rho_{0}
on the right-hand side of the same equation (recall that the
O(\rho^{\ell})
resp.
O(\rho^{\ell+1})
terms do not depend on
\rho_{0}
):

(5.47)

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{\rho^{\ell}}(\Delta^{\ell,\rho}(\rho_{0}+\rho)-\Delta^{\ell,\rho}(\rho_{0}))=\frac{1}{\rho^{\ell}}\Delta^{\ell+1,\rho}(\rho_{0})\\
\Rightarrow\qquad&c_{\ell}(\rho_{0}+\rho)-c_{\ell}(\rho_{0})=c_{\ell+1}(\rho_{0})\rho+O(\rho),\qquad\text{for }\rho>0.\end{split}

By the boundedness of
c_{\ell+1}
(
5.39
), this establishes the Lipschitz-continuity (
5.41
) of
c_{\ell}
.

Now we prove the differentiability (
5.42
): By induction, suppose that we already established that
f^{(\ell-1)}(\rho_{0})=c_{\ell-1}(\rho_{0})
for all
\rho_{0}\in(0,\infty)
, and
1\leqslant\ell<k
. Now, for
\rho>0
, one has

(5.48)

\begin{split}\Delta^{\ell-1,\rho}(\rho_{0})&=\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\binom{\ell-1}{i}(-1)^{\ell-1-i}\left\{\sum_{j=0}^{\ell-1}\frac{c_{j}(\rho_{0})}{j!}(i\rho)^{j}+\frac{c_{\ell}(\rho_{0})}{\ell!}(i\rho)^{\ell}\right\}+O(\rho^{\ell+1})\\
&=c_{\ell-1}(\rho_{0})\rho^{\ell-1}+\underbrace{\sum_{i=0}^{\ell-1}\binom{\ell-1}{i}(-1)^{\ell-1-i}\frac{i^{\ell}}{\ell!}}_{=:C(\ell)}c_{\ell}(\rho_{0})\rho^{\ell}+O(\rho^{\ell+1}),\end{split}

having used the same arguments as in the proof of (
5.44
), with
C(\ell)\in\mathbb{R}
some numerical constant. The latter gives us that for
\rho>0
,

(5.49)

\begin{split}\frac{1}{\rho^{\ell}}\Delta^{\ell,\rho}(\rho_{0})&\mathrel{{\mathop{=}\limits}}\frac{1}{\rho}\left\{\frac{1}{\rho^{\ell-1}}\Delta^{\ell-1}(\rho_{0}+\rho)-\frac{1}{\rho^{\ell-1}}\Delta^{\ell-1}(\rho_{0})\right\}\\
&=\underbrace{\frac{1}{\rho}(c_{\ell-1}(\rho_{0}+\rho)-c_{\ell-1}(\rho_{0}))}_{=\frac{1}{\rho}(f^{(\ell-1)}(\rho_{0}+\rho)-f^{(\ell-1)}(\rho_{0}))}+C(\ell)(c_{\ell}(\rho_{0}+\rho)-c_{\ell}(\rho_{0}))+O(\rho).\end{split}

On the other hand, the left-hand side of the equation above is also equal to
c_{\ell}(\rho_{0})+O(\rho)
. Letting
\rho\downarrow 0
then shows that the right-derivative of
f^{(\ell-1)}
at
\rho_{0}
exists and equals
c_{\ell}(\rho_{0})
, upon using (
5.41
) for
c_{\ell}
. Replacing
\rho_{0}
by
\rho_{0}-\rho
in (
5.49
) then gives

(5.50)

\frac{1}{\rho}(f^{(\ell-1)}(\rho_{0})-f^{(\ell-1)}(\rho_{0}-\rho))=c_{\ell}(\rho_{0}-\rho)+O(\rho),

from which one can then infer the left-derivative of
f^{(\ell-1)}
as well (using once more (
5.41
)). This finishes the proof of (
5.42
). Since
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
was arbitrary, the proof of Theorem
2.3
is complete.
∎

6.
Local uniform convergence

The aim of this section is to strengthen the pointwise convergence of the sequences
({\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{0}))_{m\geqslant 1}
and
({\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}_{*}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{0}))_{m\geqslant 1}
towards
{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}(\rho_{0})
for each fixed
\rho_{0}>0
(see (
2.5
) and below) to a locally uniform convergence, that is to show the following statement.

Proposition 6.1
.

The mappings
{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\cdot)
and
{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}_{*}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\cdot)
both converge to
{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}(\cdot)
locally uniformly over
[0,\infty)
as
m
tends to infinity. Moreover, for every
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, the sequence of approximate derivatives
c_{k,m}
converges locally uniformly to
c_{k}
, as
m
tends to infinity (recall (
5.6
) and (
2.12
) for the respective definitions).

The local uniform convergence of
{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\cdot)
and
{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}_{*}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\cdot)
could in fact be obtained as a consequence of the quantitative estimate (
2.6
) and the observation that the exponent
\alpha>0
and the constant
C<\infty
appearing there can be chosen locally uniformly over
\rho_{0}>0
. However, we think it useful to point out that Proposition
6.1
is actually a rather straightforward consequence of the qualitative statement that, for each fixed
\rho_{0}>0
,

(6.1)

{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}(\rho_{0})=\lim_{m\to\infty}{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{0})=\lim_{m\to\infty}{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}_{*}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{0}).

As will be seen, once (
6.1
) is granted, the fact that these sequences converge locally uniformly as
\rho_{0}
varies is an application of Dini’s theorem.

Since we will need to show the continuity of
{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\cdot)
, we first need to develop some version of Lemma
3.1
geared towards
\nu(U,p,\rho_{0})
instead of
\nu_{\ast}(U,q,\rho_{0})
. To state it, we define for a bounded open
U\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
the function space
\mathscr{D}(U)
to consist of sequences of functions
{f=(f_{n})_{n\geqslant 0}}
, where
f_{n}:U^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
satisfy

(1)

f_{0}
is a constant and for every
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
f_{n}\in C^{\infty}(U^{n})
;

(2)

There exists a compact set
K\subseteq U
such that for any
x_{i}\notin K

\displaystyle f_{n}(x_{1},\cdots,x_{i-1},x_{i},x_{i+1},\cdots,x_{n})=f_{n-1}(x_{1},\cdots,x_{i-1},x_{i+1},\cdots,x_{n}).

The canonical projection in (
3.2
) is an injection from
\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{c}(U)
to
\mathscr{D}(U)
; in other words, we can think of
\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{c}(U)
as a subset of
\mathscr{D}(U)
.

We then define a version of the minimization problem in the first line of (
2.3
) with
\mathscr{D}(U)
replacing
\mathscr{H}^{1}_{0}(U)
. Define for
f\in\mathscr{D}(U)
the quantity

(6.2)

\mathcal{K}(f,U,p,\rho_{0})\\
:=\frac{e^{-\rho_{0}|U|}}{2\rho_{0}|U|}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(\rho_{0}|U|)^{n}}{n!}\left(\strokedint_{U^{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(p+\nabla_{x_{i}}f_{n})\cdot\mathbf{a}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\delta_{x_{k}},x_{i}\right)(p+\nabla_{x_{i}}f_{n})\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\cdots{\mathrm{d}}x_{n}\right).

With this definition, one has the following result.

Lemma 6.2
.

For every bounded open set
U
,

\nu(U,p,\rho_{0})=\inf_{f\in\mathscr{D}(U)}\mathcal{K}(f,U,p,\rho_{0})
.

Proof.

For every
f=(f_{n})_{n\geqslant 0}\in\mathscr{D}(U)
, we consider the symmetrization
\widetilde{f}=(\widetilde{f}_{n})_{n\geqslant 0}
by defining
\widetilde{f}_{n}=\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{n}}f(x_{\sigma(1)},\cdots,x_{\sigma(n)})
. This function fulfills
\widetilde{f}\in\mathscr{D}(U)
: Indeed
\widetilde{f}_{n}\in C^{\infty}(U^{n})
follows directly from the definition, and letting
K\subseteq U
be the compact set associated with
f
, one has e.g. for the case
x_{1}\notin K

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}_{n}(x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{n})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{n}}f_{n}(x_{\sigma(1)},x_{\sigma(2)},\cdots,x_{\sigma(n)})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(n-1)!}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{n-1}(\{2,\cdots,n\})}f_{n-1}(x_{\sigma(2)},x_{\sigma(3)},\cdots,x_{\sigma(n)})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{f}_{n-1}(x_{2},\cdots,x_{n}).

Here, from the first line to the second line, we can remove
x_{1}
in the function, and make use of the natural
n
-to-
1
bijection from the group of permutations
S_{n}
to the group of permutations
S_{n-1}(\{2,\cdots,n\})
. This establishes the second condition for functions in
\mathscr{D}(U)
, so
\widetilde{f}\in\mathscr{D}(U)
.

By an application of Jensen’s inequality, it follows that

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}(\widetilde{f},U,p,\rho_{0})

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{e^{-\rho_{0}|U|}}{2\rho_{0}|U|}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(\rho_{0}|U|)^{n}}{n!}\frac{1}{n!}\sum_{\sigma\in S_{n}}\left(\strokedint_{U^{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left((p+\nabla_{x_{i}}f_{n}(x_{\sigma(1)},\cdots,x_{\sigma(n)}))\right.\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\left.\left.\cdot\mathbf{a}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\delta_{x_{k}},x_{i}\right)(p+\nabla_{x_{i}}f_{n}(x_{\sigma(1)},\cdots,x_{\sigma(n)}))\right)\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\cdots{\mathrm{d}}x_{n}\right),

which implies that
\mathcal{K}(\widetilde{f},U,p,\rho_{0})\leqslant\mathcal{K}(f,U,p,\rho_{0})
. This establishes that the value
\inf_{f\in\mathscr{D}(U)}\mathcal{K}(f,U,p,\rho_{0})
can be attained on the subspace with invariance by permutation, which can be identified as
\mathscr{C}^{\infty}_{c}(U)
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
6.1
.

We need to verify that

(6.3)

For fixed
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\cdot)
and
{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}_{*}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\cdot)
are continuous.

Once (
6.3
) is established, the uniform convergence follows from Dini’s theorem, which states that if a decreasing or increasing sequence of continuous functions
(f_{n})_{n\geqslant 1}
converges pointwisely to a continuous function
f
, then the convergence is a locally uniform. Recall that
({\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\cdot))_{m\geqslant 1}
is decreasing and
({\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}_{*}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\cdot))_{m\geqslant 1}
is increasing and the common limit (
6.1
) is ensured by
[
23
, Theorem 1.1]
. Moreover, note that
{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}(\cdot)
is continuous by Theorem
2.3
(in fact, to establish the continuity of
{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}(\cdot)
it suffices to establish its upper and lower semicontinuity, which follows from the monotone convergence of
{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\cdot)
and
{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}_{*}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\cdot)
, respectively). Therefore, it suffices to justify the continuity condition (
6.3
).

Step 1: Continuity of
{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}_{*}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\cdot)
.
The continuity of
{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}_{*}^{-1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\cdot)
follows immediately from (
4.4
), and this implies the continuity of
{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}_{*}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\cdot)
.

Step 2: Continuity of
{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\cdot)
.

We use the exact expression of the subadditive quantity

\displaystyle\nu({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},p,\rho_{0}+\rho)

\displaystyle=p\cdot{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{0}+\rho)p

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{(\rho_{0}+\rho)|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}(p+\nabla\phi^{\rho}_{m})\cdot\mathbf{a}^{\rho}(p+\nabla\phi^{\rho}_{m})\,{\mathrm{d}}(\mu+\mu_{\rho})\right],

for
m\in\mathbb{N}
,
p\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\phi^{\rho}_{m}
denotes the minimizer in the definition of
\nu({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},p,\rho_{0}+\rho)
.
We now derive an upper bound on the above expression. Using Lemma
6.2
, we know that
\phi_{m}(\mu)
is a sub-minimizer for the problem
\nu({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},p,\rho_{0}+\rho)
with density
\rho+\rho_{0}
. Also with the help of Mecke’s identity (
4.10
), we obtain that

\displaystyle p\cdot{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{0}+\rho)p

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{(\rho_{0}+\rho)|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}(p+\nabla\phi_{m}(\mu,\cdot))\cdot\mathbf{a}(\mu+\mu_{\rho},\cdot)(p+\nabla\phi_{m}(\mu,\cdot))\,{\mathrm{d}}(\mu+\mu_{\rho})\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{(\rho_{0}+\rho)|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}(p+\nabla\phi_{m}(\mu,\cdot))\cdot\mathbf{a}(\mu+\mu_{\rho},\cdot)(p+\nabla\phi_{m}(\mu,\cdot))\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]+\frac{\rho\Lambda|p|^{2}}{\rho_{0}+\rho}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{(\rho_{0}+\rho)|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}(p+\nabla\phi_{m,\xi}(\mu,\cdot))\cdot(\mathbf{a}(\mu+\mu_{\rho},\cdot)-\mathbf{a}(\mu,\cdot))(p+\nabla\phi_{m}(\mu,\cdot))\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(\frac{\rho_{0}}{\rho_{0}+\rho}\right)p\cdot{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{0})p+\left(\frac{\rho}{\rho_{0}+\rho}\right)\Lambda|p|^{2}.

For the first term, we perform an expansion with respect to
\mu_{\rho}
, and note that
\mathbf{a}(\mu+\mu_{\rho},\cdot)-\mathbf{a}(\mu)=0
on the event
\{\mu_{\rho}=0\}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{(\rho_{0}+\rho)|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}(p+\nabla\phi_{m}(\mu,\cdot))\cdot(\mathbf{a}(\mu+\mu_{\rho},\cdot)-\mathbf{a}(\mu,\cdot))(p+\nabla\phi_{m}(\mu,\cdot))\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]

\displaystyle=e^{-\rho|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{(\rho|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|)^{k}}{k!}\frac{1}{(\rho_{0}+\rho)|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.\times\strokedint_{({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m})^{k}}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}(p+\nabla\phi_{m}(\mu,\cdot))\cdot\left(\mathbf{a}(\mu+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\delta_{x_{i}},\cdot)-\mathbf{a}(\mu,\cdot)\right)(p+\nabla\phi_{m}(\mu,\cdot))\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right]\,{\mathrm{d}}x_{1}\cdots{\mathrm{d}}x_{k}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\rho|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|\left(e^{-\rho|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\frac{(\rho|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|)^{(k-1)}}{(k-1)!}\Lambda^{2}|p|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\rho|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|\Lambda^{2}|p|^{2}.

This gives us

(6.4)

p\cdot{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{0}+\rho)p-p\cdot{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{0})p\\
\leqslant\rho|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|\Lambda^{2}|p|^{2}+\left(\frac{\rho}{\rho_{0}+\rho}\right)\Lambda|p|^{2}-\left(\frac{\rho}{\rho_{0}+\rho}\right)p\cdot{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{0})p.

Taking
\rho\searrow 0
we obtain that

(6.5)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\searrow 0}{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{0}+\rho)\leqslant{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{0}).

We now establish that
\lim_{\rho\searrow 0}{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{0}+\rho)={\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{0})
. To this end, we drop out the part of integration against
\mu_{\rho}
and obtain

(6.6)

p\cdot{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{0}+\rho)p\\
\geqslant\frac{\rho_{0}}{\rho_{0}+\rho}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}(p+\nabla\phi^{\rho}_{m})\cdot\mathbf{a}(\mu+\mu_{\rho},\cdot)(p+\nabla\phi^{\rho}_{m})\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right].

We compare this with the following minimization problem, in which we fix
\mathcal{M}_{\delta}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
p\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
U\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d}
a bounded domain,

(6.7)

\displaystyle\nu(U,p;\mu_{\rho}):=\inf_{v\in\mathscr{H}^{1}_{0}(U)}\int\left(\frac{1}{\rho|U|}\int_{U}\frac{1}{2}(p+\nabla v)\cdot\mathbf{a}(\mu+\mu_{\rho},\cdot)(p+\nabla v)\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right){\mathrm{d}}\mathbb{P}_{\rho_{0}}(\mu).

This can always be seen as the problem like (
2.3
), but with a perturbation with a fixed point process
\mu_{\rho}
. We denote by
\mu\mapsto\phi_{m}(\mu;\mu_{\rho})\in\mathscr{H}^{1}_{0}(U)
its minimizer, and for every fixed
\mu_{\rho}\in\mathcal{M}_{\delta}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
\phi^{\rho}_{m}(\cdot+\mu_{\rho})
is a sub-minimizer for (
6.7
). Therefore, (
6.6
) gives that

p\cdot{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{0}+\rho)p\\
\geqslant\frac{\rho_{0}}{\rho_{0}+\rho}\mathbb{E}_{\rho_{0}}\left[\frac{1}{\rho_{0}|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}\int_{{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}}(p+\nabla\phi_{m}(\mu;\mu_{\rho}))\cdot\mathbf{a}(\mu+\mu_{\rho},\cdot)(p+\nabla\phi_{m}(\mu;\mu_{\rho}))\,{\mathrm{d}}\mu\right].

We perform an expansion with respect to
\mu_{\rho}
and notice that, when
\mu_{\rho}=0
the problem (
6.7
) is exactly the same as (
2.3
) and
{\phi_{m}(\mu;0)=\phi_{m}(\mu)}
, so we obtain that

(6.8)

\displaystyle p\cdot{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{0}+\rho)p\geqslant\left(\frac{\rho_{0}e^{-\rho|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}}{\rho_{0}+\rho}\right)p\cdot{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{0})p.

This also concludes that

(6.9)

\displaystyle\lim_{\rho\searrow 0}{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{0}+\rho)\geqslant{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{0}).

Combining (
6.9
) and (
6.5
) yields the right continuity of
{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\cdot)
.

We also need to verify the left continuity. We define
\rho_{1}:=\rho_{0}+\rho
which is fixed, then (
6.4
) becomes

\displaystyle p\cdot{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{1})p-p\cdot{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{0})p\leqslant\rho|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|\Lambda^{2}|p|^{2}+\left(\frac{\rho}{\rho_{1}}\right)\Lambda|p|^{2}-\left(\frac{\rho}{\rho_{1}}\right)|p|^{2}.

We let
\rho_{1}\nearrow\rho_{0}
and obtain that

\displaystyle{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{1})\leqslant\lim_{\rho_{0}\nearrow\rho_{1}}{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{0}).

Similarly, we put fixed
\rho_{1}=\rho_{0}+\rho
into (
6.8
) and get

\displaystyle p\cdot{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{1})p-p\cdot{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{0})p\geqslant\left(\frac{\rho_{0}e^{-\rho|{\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m}|}}{\rho_{1}}-1\right)\Lambda|p|^{2},

which means that

\displaystyle{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{1})\geqslant\lim_{\rho_{0}\nearrow\rho_{1}}{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{0}).

These prove the left continuity of
{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\cdot)
, establishing that
{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\cdot)
is continuous.

Step 3: Locally uniform convergence of
c_{k,m}
.
We now turn to the proof of the locally uniform convergence of
\{c_{k,m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
. Let
K>0
and
\rho>0
. For the case
k=1
, by (
5.8
) and (
5.40
), we find that

\displaystyle\sup_{\rho_{0}\in[0,K]}|c_{1,m}(\rho_{0})-c_{1}(\rho_{0})|

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1}{\rho}\sup_{\rho_{0}\in[0,K]}|R_{1}(m,\rho_{0},\rho)|+O(\rho)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\rho}\sup_{\rho_{0}\in[0,K]}|q\cdot({\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}^{-1}(\rho_{0}+\rho)-{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}_{*}^{-1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{0}+\rho))q|

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\rho}\sup_{\rho_{0}\in[0,K]}|q\cdot({\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}^{-1}(\rho_{0})-{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}_{*}^{-1}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\rho_{0}))q|.

Using the statement of Proposition
5.2
, the first line on the right-hand side of the previous display is uniformly bounded by a constant
O(\rho)
independent of
m
and
\rho_{0}
, and the locally uniform convergence of
({\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}_{*}({\mathchoice{\scalebox{1.2}{$\displaystyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\textstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptstyle\square$}}{\scalebox{1.2}{$\scriptscriptstyle\square$}}}_{m},\cdot))_{m\geqslant 1}
towards
{\overbracket{\mathbf{a}}}
makes the second line and third line vanish when
m
tends to infinity. Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle\limsup_{m\to\infty}\sup_{\rho_{0}\in[0,K]}|c_{1,m}(\rho_{0})-c_{1}(\rho_{0})|\leqslant O(\rho).

Since the left hand side of the display above does not depend on
\rho
, we can let
\rho
be arbitrarily small, which proves the locally uniform convergence of
\{c_{1,m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
. For the case
k\geqslant 2
, the claim about
\{c_{k,m}\}_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
follows in the same manner by induction.
∎
